## v5 Phase 2: answers as they land, "applied" on more platforms, and the page's own world while it fills (docs/SPEC-AMENDMENTS.md §15)
What follows holds over every note above and below where they differ, "v5 Phase 1" included. The plan's Phase 2, under the owner's "Implement like jobright. I approved all" (2026-11-08), built by three builders on main `019f489`: **P2-stream** (`038f867`, merged `c1b0399`, with TailorIT's stream route on `v1.2-p13` `3888a46`; its follow-up `2369d75`, one round for open questions and two AI tasks per run, with TailorIT `9d77411`), **P2-detect** (`addfa31` and `1b19691`; its follow-up `983c81e` and `5681947`, merged `562960a`, after the owner's word on Jobright's build: "Yes, Jobright is sample, and it works perfectly. you can use it") and **P2-fill** (`1037496`, which took each of the others in turn: `16cc5a0` moved its page patches out of the page-world bridge into a script of their own; on main as `788bf31`). Jobright 1.25.2 was read for behaviour only: where no snapshot of ours shows a page, a sign or address shape taken from Jobright's observed behaviour is written in our own code and words, its comment names the Jobright module, rule or registry it was read from, and it is tested on a synthetic page of our own (docs/FIXTURES.md); none of its code, CSS, assets or wording is copied. Decisions taken under "I approved all": docs/OWNER-QUESTIONS.md §16.
**The Phase 2 review's fixes** (2026-11-08; docs/SPEC-AMENDMENTS.md §15 "Review fixes (Phase 2)"), on main `6611481`: P2-detect `8363026` and `a5ee006`, P2-stream `dce1fe3` (with TailorIT `v1.2-p13` `31296c8`) and P2-fill `d4c2904`. Each is folded into the notes below where it changes them ("since the review", with the review's item), and "The Phase 2 review's fixes" below gathers what they add.

### AI answers as they land (P2-stream; `background/server-client.ts`, `ai-tasks.ts`, `planner.ts`; `content/runtime.ts`, `orchestrator.ts`, `loop.ts`; `core/loop/bookkeeping.ts`)
The engine audit's J3, J9, J16 and gap 2: Jobright gets every answer of a form from one streamed request. Ours keeps its own split (profile values planned in the worker, only the open questions sent to TailorIT's AI) and streams those.
- **TailorIT (API 6, additive; built on its side):** `GET /api/ai/tasks/:id/stream` (`text/event-stream`, scope `ai:run`, GET only) sends each answer of an answer task and each draft of a draft task as TailorIT settles it, while Claude still writes the rest, then `event: task` with the finished `AiTaskView`, then ends; `: ping` every `AUTOFILL_AI_STREAM_HEARTBEAT_MS` (10 s); its own cap is twice the CLI timeout plus 60 s (it then ends without the task, and the caller polls; since the review's security 6a at most 150 s, with at most six streams open per account and two per task, past which it answers 429 `rate_limited`); `Cache-Control: no-store, no-transform` and `X-Accel-Buffering: no` keep any proxy from holding it back. A pick or a classification streams no item (its stream carries the finished task alone). An item is released once: when it names a new item of the request, the reply up to its end does not talk as an AI, and its own checks pass (the finished task's checks, item by item); the first entry that breaks the reply's rules stops the release, and the rest is judged whole as before. A released item is final: the finished task holds it unchanged. **A request carrying canaries never streams an item** (its reply is judged whole, so a trap's canary anywhere still discards every answer). `Prefer: respond-async` (RFC 7240) on an AI route answers 202 at once with `Preference-Applied`. Contract: `AutofillTaskStreamEventSchema` (`answer` | `draft` | `task`), `AUTOFILL_AI_STREAM_EVENTS`, `AUTOFILL_AI_STREAM_HEARTBEAT_MS`, `AUTOFILL_AI_RESPOND_ASYNC`; examples under `fixtures/AutofillTaskStreamEvent/`; the extension pulled it from TailorIT's worktree (`npm run contract:pull`). An older TailorIT answers the stream 404 and the extension polls as before.
- **The worker:** while it reads streams, a batch is asked with `Prefer: respond-async` and its task's stream read (`server-client.ts` `aiTaskStream`): its headers within the usual 25 s, then for as long as the task's 120 s cap and the run allow, each read within `AI_STREAM_IDLE_MS` (25 s, two and a half heartbeats), at most `AI_STREAM_MAX_BYTES` (2 MiB), every message checked against the contract (its `event:` name the JSON's `type`; one that breaks it ends the stream as a bad answer). Each streamed answer or draft is that key's verdict at once (`answerVerdicts` and `draftVerdicts` over that one item, the finished task's translation), its key done before its batch, and the run's `onReady` hears it; the planner then sends `plan/ready {runId}` to the run's own frame and document (one message per burst; since the review's security 5 it may also carry `reviews`, the keys whose streamed reply TailorIT then voided, never an answer). Polling stays the fallback: a stream that ends without its task (cut, idle, the cap) is polled within the same cap; a stream answered 404 while its task answers a poll means a TailorIT without it, and the worker polls for the rest of its life (`ai.stream-unsupported`); any other failure polls. A key that came on the stream keeps its verdict when its task then fails; a finished task that differs from what streamed (TailorIT promises it never does) wins for later plans, counted (`ai.stream-differs`). The run's end aborts its stream.
- **The frame:** `plan/ready` is taken from the worker alone (refused from a page or an extension page) and only for the frame's current run; it rings the loop's `AiWake`, so the wait for the AI polls at once (a ring between a poll and the wait after it is kept). The `AI_POLL_MS` tick (1.5 s) stays, as the fallback and to keep the worker awake; `content.ai-waited` counts the wakes (`woken`).
- **The pass caps:** answers now come a few at a time, each burst a pass, so a pass that only writes answers that came in (no field planned, none held back) counts against neither `MAX_PASSES` (8) nor `MAX_AI_PASSES` (20) (`noteArrivalPass`, `LoopState.arrivalPasses`); `MAX_LOOP_PASSES` (80) still ends any loop.
- **One round for open questions** (the follow-up): before, a question no rule classified went to a classify task and, only once it came back unknown, to an answer task: two CLI calls in a row. Now (`PlanInput.oneRound`, which the worker passes unless TailorIT showed it cannot) `core/plan/frame.ts` holds such a field (`unclassified`) and, at the answer step, where that step would ask it once classified unknown, sends its answer item with `classify: true` and its frame's platform; otherwise (a draft took it, a list of one option, a format no answer takes, AI answers off, a dry run) it is classified first, as before. Contract, additive within API 6: `AutofillAnswerItem.classify`, `AutofillAnswerRequest.platform`, `AutofillAnswer.classification {category, reason}`; TailorIT's answer prompt (version 5) carries the classify list when an item asks, and an item classified into a category whose answer is never kept is answered `sensitive`. The worker asks such items in one batch per platform (an item of a platform the contract does not name, `site`, as a plain answer, its classification unknown at once); the answer's classification becomes the key's classification verdict (`oneRoundClassifications`), and its answer verdict is marked `oneRound` with its scope. **The category decides first** (the answer step): a category the profile fills takes the profile's value and the one-round answer is never used (where the profile holds none, today's own ask follows); a self-identification, legal, pay or consent category is the person's (the answer dropped; the `all` scope's legal guess asked again under that scope, as before); unknown takes the answer where the answer step would ask it under the same scope. An answer without the classification it was asked for (a TailorIT before this) is dropped unread, and the worker classifies first for the rest of its life (`ai.one-round-unsupported`).
- **Two tasks at once** (the follow-up): `createRunAi` keeps `AI_RUN_TASKS` (2) tasks in flight per run, in the order their batches were asked (`idle()` waits for all). TailorIT runs at most `AI_CLI_PER_ACCOUNT` CLI processes of one account at once, the account's other tasks waiting their turn and another account's never waiting for them (since the review's M2 its default, empty or 0, is the whole `AI_CLI_CONCURRENCY`, so on a one-person server the limit applies only when set, and background work never takes an account place); the contract's two queued or running tasks per kind (`AUTOFILL_AI_MAX_ACTIVE_PER_KIND`) are unchanged, so a second tab's run of the same kind still meets `ai_busy` and waits.
- **Measured** (e2e `61-ai-stream`, the synthetic sixty-field form: 24 profile fields and 36 questions only the AI answers; the mock pacing each task as a CLI writes, a start then a time per item; times from the run's first write; each build an e2e build against the same mock):

  | pace | build | first AI answer | last AI answer | report at rest |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | 1.0 s + 0.2 s | before Phase 2 (`019f489`), 2 runs | 9.6 / 11.2 s | 22.3 / 20.8 s | 23.0 / 21.6 s |
  | 1.0 s + 0.2 s | the stream alone (`c1b0399`), 2 runs | 8.8 / 8.6 s | 19.7 / 19.7 s | 20.5 / 20.2 s |
  | 1.0 s + 0.2 s | one round and two tasks (`2369d75`), streamed, 2 runs | 0.79 / 0.75 s | 6.8 / 6.9 s | 7.3 / 7.7 s |
  | 1.0 s + 0.2 s | one round and two tasks, stream off (polling), 2 runs | 1.8 / 1.8 s | 6.6 / 6.7 s | 7.2 / 7.7 s |
  | 3.0 s + 0.6 s | before Phase 2 (`019f489`) | 30.9 s | 61.7 s | 62.5 s |
  | 3.0 s + 0.6 s | the stream alone (`c1b0399`) | 26.1 s | 59.3 s | 60.0 s |
  | 3.0 s + 0.6 s | one round and two tasks, streamed | 3.5 s | 21.3 s | 23.2 s |
  | 3.0 s + 0.6 s | one round and two tasks, stream off (polling) | 7.1 s | 21.5 s | 21.9 s |

  The stream fills answers one by one within a task, but alone it saved only the polls between tasks; the time to done was the chain of tasks (two classifications, then four answer tasks, one at a time). One round and two tasks at once cut that chain.
- **The fresh scan before each write** (`content/pass.ts` → `dom/guard.ts` `domGuardBeforeWrite` → `last.rescan()`): measured, not changed. One scan of the sixty-field form takes about 13.5 ms (79 % of it labels and descriptions); a fill writing 56 fields made 64 scans, 0.84 s of a 2.3 s fill with instant answers: a large share of the CPU, little of a run whose AI answers take seconds. Reusing a scan across writes would weaken the guard's rule (never a cached node), since our own write can change what CSS shows (`:checked`, `:has()`, `:focus-within`) with no DOM change (docs/OWNER-QUESTIONS.md §16).

### "Applied" on more platforms (P2-detect; `src/dom/submit-net-protocol.ts`, `src/page/submit-watch.ts`, `src/entrypoints/submit-watch.content.ts`, `src/adapters/success.ts`)
PLAN decision 14 (main-world patches), taken for "applied" detection; the engine audit's J11 and gap 3. No snapshot of ours shows any of these platforms' pages after submitting (a scan of every owner snapshot found only Breezy's, Pinpoint's and iCIMS's, already used), so each sign below is an address pattern, the platform's own request that sends the application, or, since the follow-up, a page rule read from Jobright's behaviour.
- **The page-world submit watcher:** a third static content script, world MAIN, `all_frames`, `document_start`, matched on `SUBMIT_WATCH_MATCHES` (`shared/hosts.ts`, each one of `ATS_MATCHES`: Workday's `*.myworkdayjobs.com`, `*.myworkdaysite.com` and `*.myworkdayjobs-impl.com`, ClearCompany's `/careers/` pages, Oracle Recruiting Cloud's Candidate Experience on `*.oraclecloud.com` and `*.fa.ocs.oraclegovcloud.com`, and SuccessFactors' `*.successfactors.com` and `*.successfactors.eu`). It installs where the page-world bridge would (`bridgeInstallsAt` and `framedByEmployerPage`, decided once from the address the document was loaded at) and, since the review's M3 (`adapters/bridge-address.ts` `submitWatchInstallsAt`), anywhere under a single-page candidate app's own path that a person enters at a job list: Oracle's `/hcmUI/CandidateExperience/`, Workday's `myworkdaysite.com` under `/[<locale>/]recruiting/<tenant>/<site>` (a tenant's own host was already any path) and ClearCompany's `/careers/` (SuccessFactors as the bridge: its career site loads a document per page); never where `framedByEmployerPage` says no. Only on a platform with a network sign (`NET_PLATFORMS`: workday, clearcompany, oracle, successfactors); elsewhere it does nothing at all. On Workday, ClearCompany and Oracle it wraps `window.fetch` and `XMLHttpRequest.prototype.open` and `send`, always calling the page's own with exactly what the page gave and handing back exactly what it returned (no header read, nothing retried or delayed; it never throws into the page). For a request whose method and address are the platform's own send request (`netRequestOf`: https, the page's own origin, no user name or fragment, the platform's path and query shape) it waits for the answer, reads the one fact the sign needs and dispatches `tailorit:submit-net` on the document, its detail the string `{"v":1,"sign":"<sign>"}` (a string crosses the worlds; an object does not). An XHR's request waits in a WeakMap from `open` to `send`, and its answer's own address is read again (a redirect elsewhere is no sign). It holds no address, id or value; what it reads of a body (at most `NET_BODY_MAX`, 64 KiB, parsed as JSON field by field) is dropped at once.
- **The signs** (`NET_SIGNS`, taken in each platform's order, `NET_SEQUENCES`):
  - **Workday** `workday-finalize`: POST `/wday/cxs/<tenant>/jobapplication/<id>/finalize`, no query or exactly the agency submission's `type`, answered 200, not redirected, its answer a JSON object naming the application (a non-empty `id`) with no `error` or `errors` (read from a copy: `Response.clone().text()`, or the XHR's own text or parsed JSON).
  - **ClearCompany** `clearcompany-responses` then `clearcompany-completed`: the last page's answers (POST `.../apply/form/<uuid>/<uuid>/responses/<uuid>` with exactly its two `false` flags and an optional page number) answered 200, then the completed workflow's message (GET `.../form/<uuid>/message/completedMessage/<lang>`, at most `reqId`, `candidateId` and a cache stamp) answered 200, which the page asks for only once the workflow ended. Status only: its bodies are never touched.
  - **Oracle Recruiting Cloud** `oracle-created` then `oracle-confirmed`: two POSTs to `/hcmRestApi/resources/<version|latest>/recruitingCEJobApplications` on the page's origin, no query, told apart by the request's own body (the string the page sends, or a copy of its Request): its `action` (CREATE or CONFIRM) for the posting the page's address shows (`requisitionNumber` the address's job id, a candidate number named). A 2xx CREATE answer naming a `submissionId` is the first; a 2xx CONFIRM answer saying `confirmedFlag` and `activeFlag` true for the same posting the second. (Jobright also matches the CONFIRM's candidate to the CREATE's; ours keeps no candidate number: the order and the page's posting stand in.)
  - **SuccessFactors** `successfactors-applied`: its DWR call answers 200 whatever happened, so the watcher wraps the page's own `rcmHybridJobApplicationControllerASProxy.applyJob` once the page made it (looked for every 250 ms for a minute); for this posting's new application (the call's `jobReqId` the address's `career_job_req_id` or `jobReqId`, else the form's job id box; `appId` -1; not `viewApplicationMode`) it wraps the callback the page gave, still calling it with DWR's own result, and tells the sign when that result says `success` true with an action response object.
- **The isolated world** (`content/submitted.ts` `watchNetworkSigns`; `content/runtime.ts` `arm`, its `// v5 p2-detect` block): only while a Submit is armed (the person's trusted click on the adapter's Submit, the panel's press, or Submit mode Auto's) does the document listen for the event, and only on a platform with a network sign (no listener anywhere else, and none before an arm); it parses the detail strictly (`parseNetSign`: exactly `{v: 1, sign}`, at most 64 characters) and takes the platform's signs in order (a sign out of turn changes nothing). Once they are all in, and only while the page shows no error (`press.ts` `refusalNow`), the arm's watch ends and `submit/detected` goes with evidence `'network'` (`messaging/schemas.ts` `SUBMIT_EVIDENCE`).
- **The worker** (`background/submissions.ts`, its `// v5 p2-detect` block): a network sign counts only from the frame **and** document that armed the Submit, within `SUBMIT_ARM_MS`, once; then POST /applied as for any sign.
- **Oracle on an employer's own domain** (the follow-up; never a static match): `shared/hosts.ts` `platformOfUrl` reads a host no `HOST_PLATFORMS` entry names, on a path `/hcmUI/CandidateExperience/<lang>/sites/<site>/` (`ORACLE_OWN_DOMAIN_PATH`, from Jobright's site registry; since the review's security 1 that prefix alone, the one the registry and every Oracle fixture name; `ORACLE_CANDIDATE_APP` is `/hcmUI/CandidateExperience/`), as Oracle's (`oracleOwnDomain`), never on `ORACLE_REFUSED_DOMAINS` (Oracle's own clouds, LinkedIn's, every employee HR system's, the CAPTCHA vendors' and google.com; `adapters/oracle/url.ts` reads the same list, held equal by `shared/hosts.test.ts`), nor where `isLinkedInHost`, `isHrHost` or the vendor test say no. With no `ATS_MATCHES` entry, `onAtsMatch` stays false, so such a page is a company page: the sentinel recognises it (`dom/page-signals.ts` `jobSignalOf`: `platform`; since the review only once the page also shows Oracle's own app, "The Phase 2 review's fixes" below), `adapters/index.ts` `adaptersFor(url, doc)` puts Oracle's adapter before the site adapter there (only this case changes; since the review only with that markup), and `oracle/url.ts` keeps the company's host in the canonical address (Jobright's `jobs/preview/<id>` spelling read as `job/<id>`). The worker runs the submit watcher in that document's own world (`background/sites.ts` `watch`: `scripting.executeScript` with `world: 'MAIN'`, `SUBMIT_WATCH_FILE`; `platform/types.ts` `inject` takes `world?: 'MAIN'`); since the review not at recognition but once the content script asks (`site/watch`), after Oracle's adapter found the application form there; a refusal is logged and changes nothing.
- **Pages after submitting** (the follow-up; `adapters/success.ts`): `SuccessRule {css, text?, companion?, exclude?, from}` and `successSignsOf(doc, rules)`: an element a person sees whose own words (no scripts or styles, white space folded) read `text`, beside a visible companion whose words read its own (inside the element's parent, or `scope`), with no visible `exclude` on the page; each rule's `from` names the Jobright rule it was read from. `AtsConfig.success` is now a `SuccessRule` (`configuredSuccessSigns`). Built: Workday's (`WORKDAY_SUCCESS`: the candidate home's task dialog that says the application was submitted, with its return-home button, and the sign-in page's congratulations above the same words), SuccessFactors' (`SUCCESSFACTORS_SUCCESS`: the page title block's success message beside its jobs-applied button), BambooHR's (a "thank you" heading above the submitted message, in its config) and JazzHR's (its centred heading saying the application was received, in its config). `content/submitted.ts` `signsOf` reads Workday's and SuccessFactors' rules, the configured platforms' through their configs; every existing rule still applies (after an armed Submit, new, where the form was, outside the posting's text). None for Taleo Enterprise (Jobright's one rule is Taleo Business Edition's, a product we do not match), UltiPro (Jobright lists none), HiBob (not in Jobright) or company `site` pages (Jobright's per-company rules need each company's own Submit read first: a Phase 4 item).
- **SuccessFactors' application form** (the follow-up; `adapters/successfactors/index.ts`, from Jobright's reading alone): `form#careerform` holding a `career_ns` box valued `job_application` is the 'application' view (claimed, no auth state, filled as the standard reading fills it); its Submit (`submitButton`) is the page's own button-role span whose id ends `_submitBtn` (not aria-disabled), else the form's submit control valued "apply"; `isFinalStep` stays 'unknown', so nothing of ours presses it: it is read so the person's own click arms the watch. The form at `/portalcareer` (no job in its address) stays unkeyed, as before: no arm there.
- **Confirmation addresses** (each platform's `url.ts`, `ApplicationPage` 'confirmation' under the form's key, so the confirmation page's key is the arm's and the existing rule applies: it counts from the frame that pressed, within 60 s, on a new document or an in-document move), each Jobright's recorded route for the platform's page after submitting, read as an address pattern: Rippling (the form's own path with exactly one `step=confirmation`), CareerPlug (`/jobs/<id>/apps/<n>` after `/jobs/<id>/apps/new`), Paylocity (`/recruiting/jobs/success/<id>[/...]`, either case), SmartRecruiters (`.../oneclick-ui/company/<co>/publication/<uuid>/success`), Paycor (`/career/ShowConfirmation.action?clientId=&id=`, the form's two ids), Jobvite (`/<co>/job/<id>/applyConfirmation`) and Dayforce (`[/<locale>]/<ns>/<site>/jobs/<id>/apply/success`). `tailoritAddressOf` names each by the form's canonical address, which TailorIT's `parseAtsUrl` reads.
- **JazzHR's page after submitting** (the follow-up): `jazzhr/url.ts` reads `/apply/confirm/<code>` (no `c` in its query) as the confirmation of posting `<code>`; it names no slug, so its own key is not the form's. `adapters/address.ts` `confirmsApplication(application, confirmation)` (same platform's parser, same host, the first a posting or form and the second a confirmation, the same board in any case and the same posting id) lets `background/submissions.ts` (marked block) give a `confirmation-page` sign whose own key holds no arm to the tab's newest arm from the same frame, not yet confirmed, within `CONFIRMATION_PAGE_MS` (60 s), whose kept posting address the page confirms (`confirmedArm`); it is recorded under that arm's key.
- **Address variants** (the engine audit's gap 1a; `shared/hosts.ts` `ATS_MATCHES` and `HOST_PLATFORMS`, each platform's `url.ts`, `adapters/bridge-address.ts`), from Jobright's site registry: Workday's `*.myworkdayjobs-impl.com` (a tenant's pre-production host, read as a tenant's own host); Pinpoint's postings under any locale or none (`PINPOINT_PATH`; the key folds the locale, `/en/` canonical); Paylocity's `/recruiting/` in either case (canonical `/Recruiting/Jobs/Apply/<id>`); UKG's other recruiting hosts, since the review's security 2 by name: `recruiting2.ultipro.com` and `recruiting.ultipro.ca` whole (candidate hosts, as `recruiting.ultipro.com`) and `<x>.rec.pro.ukg.net` on its job boards' paths alone (`ULTIPRO_BOARD_PATH`, `/<tenant>/JobBoard/`); an employer's own `<n>.ultipro.com` or `<n>.ultipro.ca` is its UKG Pro employee system, job boards for its staff included, which stays no platform's, and `HR_HOST_MATCHES` keeps the sentinel off `*.ultipro.com`, `*.ultipro.ca` (added by the review) and `*.ukg.net` (`ultipro/url.ts` `RECRUITING_HOSTS` and `OTHER_HOST`; each host its own key; `parseUltiproUrl` joins the bridge's `SHARED_DOMAIN_PARSERS`, whose `PUBLIC_JOB_HOSTS` gain the two recruiting hosts); CareerPlug's partner domains (`CAREERPLUG_PARTNER_HOST`: `sfagentjobs.com`, `sfagentcareers.com`, `apscareerportal.com`, under `/jobs/`; the board is the host's first label); and (the follow-up) Dayforce's posting page `[/<locale>]/<ns>/<site>/jobs/<id>[/apply]` and Paycor's `JobIntroduction.action` posting with its `SubmitQuestionnaire.action` and `SubmitVevraaForm.action` steps, each under the form's key. `onAtsMatch` reads a `*` inside a pattern's path as Chrome does (`globPath`, held to wxt's `MatchPattern` by `shared/hosts.test.ts`); `HOST_PLATFORMS` entries take `pathPattern` beside `paths`.
- **Not built** (each for its reason; docs/OWNER-QUESTIONS.md §16): Greenhouse's EU hosts (a cross-cutting change left for its own item), Breezy's other `/apply` paths (no shape beyond the pattern), `*.rippling-ats.com` (HiringThing's, by Jobright's own registry), ClearCompany's legacy `hrmdirect.com` posting page and `smartr.me` (a posting page and a redirect), ADP MyJobs and `recruiting.adp.com` and Dayforce's sign-in pages (other applications), `*.myworkday.com` and `*.sapsf.com` (employee systems; Jobright shows no candidate shape there), Workday's `/jobTasks/completed/application` (names no job; the network sign covers Workday), BrassRing (no platform of ours). SmartRecruiters' one-click `/oneclick-ui/company/<co>/job/<id>` was on this list (its relation to the posting's key unknown) until jr6's SR-14 keyed it on its own canonical address (`smartrecruiters/url.ts`; `src/adapters/url.test.ts` `AHEAD_OF_CONTRACT`).
- **TailorIT's side (API 7, not built):** `parseAtsUrl` reads none of `<tenant>[.wd<n>].myworkdayjobs-impl.com`, UKG's other hosts' job boards, CareerPlug's partner domains, nor Oracle's Candidate Experience on an employer's own domain (the host kept). Until it does, a lookup there finds no job by address and POST /applied with `{url}` answers 404 (nothing recorded) unless the person chose the job; `src/adapters/url.test.ts` `AHEAD_OF_CONTRACT` lists those rows, each to be removed once API 7 reads it. Pinpoint's other locales, Paylocity's lower-case path, Dayforce's posting and Paycor's other pages need nothing: the extension asks with their canonical address.

### The page's own world while it fills, rich-text boxes, the résumé first and a posting's Apply (P2-fill)
The engine audit's J4, J5, J18 and gaps 6 and 11; PLAN decision 14 for the patches.
- **The page patches** (`src/entrypoints/page-patches.ts`, an unlisted script; `src/page/patches.ts` `installPatches`, `page/dialogs.ts` `dialogHold`, `page/picker.ts` `pickerArm`; `src/dom/patch-protocol.ts`; `src/dom/patches.ts` `askPatches`; `background/page-patches.ts` `createPagePatches`, `PAGE_PATCHES_FILE`, `PAGE_PATCHES_LIMIT`; `content/page-patches.ts` `loadPagePatches`, `pagePatchesSettled`). No content script: as the first run that writes begins in a document (`runtime.ts` `startRun`, never a dry run), the frame asks `page/patches`, and the worker runs the bundle in that one document, world MAIN (`scripting.executeScript` with its document id; `inject` gained `world`), only for a frame of ours that said hello with a form from that same document, never from the sentinel, at most 20 a minute per frame. Installed, the patches keep one capture listener on the document for their command (`tailorit:patch`, on the bridge's protocol rules: a JSON detail `{v, id, op}`, answered inside the dispatch; a second run of the bundle finds the first answering a probe and adds nothing) and patch nothing until a write asks. A write waits for the request's answer `PAGE_PATCHES_WAIT_MS` (1.5 s) at most and goes on either way: without the patches nothing is held and no picker control is pressed. A page where no fill runs holds none of it, not even the listener. They are not in the page-world bridge, which P2-detect's address variants left 631 bytes short of its budget.
- **The page's dialogs held through a write** (`dom/fillers/dialogs.ts` `holdDialogs`, `withDialogsHeld`, `withDialogsHeldWhile`; `page/dialogs.ts`; `content/pass.ts` `PassEnv.dialogsHeld`; `content/orchestrator.ts`; `content/ui/toasts.ts`): around each native write's own synchronous events (`fill()`), the page's `window.alert` and `window.confirm` are held (`dialogs.hold`, then `dialogs.release`, sent on the document's root element): a held alert returns as one dismissed does, a held confirm false (its Cancel, which commits to nothing; the write's read-back then says whether the page kept the value), each counted; nothing of the person's can come between those events. Through each wait (the date parts' write, a picker's press and wait, an upload's wait for the page's word) only the alert is held, since the review's security 4 (`dialogs.hold-alerts`): a confirm is a question only the person answers, so one asked during a wait (a session's "Stay signed in?") always reaches them as the browser's own dialog. Through a wait, too, the person's own trusted pointerdown, mousedown, touchstart or keydown anywhere in the document, heard first in the capture phase, ends the hold at once, so the dialog their action brings shows. (The browser's user activation cannot tell them apart: the person's click on the sidebar that starts the fill gives the page activation for seconds, and so does Playwright's driving; e2e `63-rich-text` caught it.) A release ends the latest hold, the page's own descriptors put back once none stands (a replacement the page made meanwhile kept); a hold never released ends by itself after `DIALOGS_HOLD_MS` (12 s). Counts come back, never words: logged (`content.dialogs-held {alerts, confirms}`) and, since the review (security 4, L4), told the person in a branded toast of kind `dialogs` (role `status`, `PAGE_COPY` `toasts.dialogs`: how many messages the page showed and questions it asked while Autofill filled it, closed or answered Cancel; for the run), under the person's "TailorIT notifications on the page" (none under Never show them). Not held: a widget's choice (dom/widgets), the waits for options or the AI.
- **The page's own file picker** (`Adapter.uploadPickers`; `dom/scan.ts`: a declared, visible control is a `file` field, `FieldTarget.picker`; `dom/fillers/picker.ts` `fillThroughPicker`; `page/picker.ts`; `content/pass.ts`; `content/verify.ts`): for a résumé button its adapter declares (one with no file input in the form), the control is armed (`picker.arm`), pressed with an untrusted `click()` through the never-submit door, and what the page opens is waited for (`PICKER_WAIT_MS`, 1.5 s; the page's side holds the arm `PICKER_ARM_MS`, 2 s, at most, and only until its first use). While armed, the page world's `HTMLInputElement.prototype.click` (and `window.showOpenFilePicker` where the browser has it) is shadowed. Since the review's security 7c the arm is made on the control's scope (`fillers/picker.ts` `pickerScopeOf`: what its adapter declares, `Adapter.uploadPickerScope`, from the same snapshot, else the control's parent), and only a `click()` on a file input inside that scope (`page/picker.ts` `inScope`, open shadow roots included), or on one made at the press and never put in the page, opens no picker; any other file input's goes to the page as always; and the person's own trusted pointerdown, mousedown, touchstart or keydown during the wait disarms at once. Such an input is told back on the scope (`PICKED`, a FocusEvent: the one event kind that carries an element across the worlds, as its relatedTarget), and the isolated world puts the planned file in as any file input gets it (`fillers/file.ts`: the accept list, DataTransfer, input and change) and reads it back; the upload's wait (`awaitUpload`) reads that input. A `showOpenFilePicker()` during the arm is answered with the file alone (`SUPPLY` on the scope: a detached input holding it; the page's promise resolves with one handle) and flagged upload-not-confirmed, since nothing of ours can read it back. Without the patches nothing is pressed and the row is the person's. Disarmed whatever happens (`picker.disarm`), the shadowed properties put back as they were. **No adapter declares one yet**: the read-only capture of HiBob's "Add file" opened nothing (docs/FIXTURES.md).
- **Rich-text boxes** (`dom/kinds.ts` `isEditingHost`, `editableKind`, `EDITABLE_SELECTOR`; `dom/values.ts` `editableVisibleText`, `editableHidesText`, `editableText`, `textOfControl`, `readBackTextOf`; `dom/visible-text.ts` `VisibleTextOptions.editable`; `dom/scan.ts`; `dom/fillers/text.ts` `fillEditable`, `selectEditable`; `dom/fillers/events.ts` `EDITING_EVENTS`. Since the review a box's text is read through `textOfControl`, the visible reading, by `currentValueOf` (so the scan and the loop's read-back hashes) and `dom/resume-parse.ts`; through `readBackTextOf`, `innerText`, by `dom/fillers/verify.ts` and `content/pass.ts`'s `textBefore`; and through `editableText` directly by `fillEditable`): an editing host by the HTML standard (contenteditable `""`, `true` or `plaintext-only`, editable now, its parent not) is a text field of its own, `textarea`, or `text` where it says `aria-multiline="false"`; labelled, judged visible and camouflaged by the same rules as any control; one a person cannot see is a honeypot (counted in `hiddenControlsIgnored`, never a proxy); an editable element inside another box is that box's content. Its text, as the scan, the planner and the AI read it, is what a person can see of it (since the review's security 3: `values.ts` `editableVisibleText`, the `visibleText` walker run from its editing host down with `editable: true`, still the only producer of `VisibleText`; without that option an editable region is never read, as before); `innerText` (`editableText`, `readBackTextOf`: non-breaking spaces as spaces, trailing white space dropped, white space alone empty) is our own write's read-back alone, in the frame's memory. The write: since the review's L6 the pass first selects the box's whole content and waits until its editor can have seen that selection (`selectEditable`: past the document's next `selectionchange` and one task more, at most `SELECTION_SEEN_MS`, 100 ms, and never past the run; `guard.revert` still writes at once); the box takes the focus (`preventScroll`), its whole content is the selection (kept where it already is, made where not), and its text is replaced a line at a time (`insertText` for a line, `insertParagraph` between lines); each insert is first offered as an untrusted, cancelable `beforeinput` naming it (an editor that keeps its own model takes and cancels it), and where none did, `document.execCommand` makes it with the browser's own trusted `input` and DOM change, which ProseMirror, Quill, Draft and Lexical read as typing. Then `blur()`, the selection cleared, and a read-back at once: a box that took nothing is needs-your-click; one whose editor took an insert but has not drawn it yet (Lexical's microtask) is written, not verified, and the pass's verification after its settle decides. Chrome fires no `beforeinput` for `execCommand`, so our offer is the only one an editor sees; the touch log already reads a trusted input without the person's intent as no edit (the Phase 6 review's E3), and `lastWriteAt` is taken after the write, so the run's own insert is never the person's touch. No site's selector is read: no fixture or snapshot of ours holds a contenteditable application box.
- **The résumé first where the page parses it** (`Adapter.quirks.resumeParse`; `core/plan/order.ts` `resumeFirst`, `isResumeWrite`; `content/loop.ts` `queue`, one marked line; `dom/resume-parse.ts` `awaitResumeParse`; `content/pass.ts`): declared for Rippling (its snapshots say the résumé will be parsed to fill the application) and iCIMS (its profile frames say a résumé provided there replaces the form's data, and show a parsing message while it works). A pass there writes the résumé before every other write (a cover letter is no résumé), and after its upload's wait it waits for the parse: the form's values (memory only; file inputs left out) are compared every `PARSE_POLL_MS` (100 ms), with the page's busy signs (`dom/settle.ts` `busySigns`: `aria-busy`, a progress bar, and the adapter's own, iCIMS's parsing message through its `isBusy`): `none` when nothing changed and nothing showed busy within `PARSE_IDLE_MS` (since the review's L1 8 s, was 3 s: a page whose parse shows no sign, Rippling's, fills the form only once its server read the file; a busy sign ends that window as soon as it shows), `parsed` once the values changed or a busy sign showed and then a quiet window passed (`PARSE_QUIET_MS`, 0.8 s, once a busy sign showed; `PARSE_SETTLE_MS`, 1.5 s, where none did, since a parse may fill the form in batches), `timed-out` at `PARSE_CAP_MS` (20 s), `aborted` with the run (iCIMS's reload ends the document). Since the review, when the parse changed the form, the loop reads it again before the pass's other writes (`pass.ts` `WriteResult.reread`, `loop.ts`), so `prefillOf` reconciles what the parse put in: a structured value we write over is a flagged `prefill-conflict` overwrite, as on later passes, and parsed prose is kept (`prefill-kept`); the rest is written over the parse. With one round, the AI's answers land in passes after the résumé's, so they too go over the parse (`p2-fill-run.browser.test.ts`'s Rippling case: nothing but the résumé is written before it). Polling, not a MutationObserver: a value a page's script sets changes no attribute.
- **A posting's own Apply, at the person's click** (`content/runtime.ts` `entryOffered`, `wallNow`, `sendWall`, `atWall`; `frame/wall`'s `entry`; `background/frames.ts` `FrameEntry.entry`, `setWall`, `wallOf`; `background/index.ts`; `background/panel.ts` `fill`; `PageState.entry`; `ui/panel/Panel.tsx` `MainMeaning` 'open', `MainButton`, `PageStrip`): the top (or claim) frame of a posting page with no form tells the worker, at each change, whether an adapter of its address offers its own Apply now (`Adapter.entryControl`, a control a person can see: each platform's declaration, from its fixtures). P2-fill gave the panel's main button there a name of its own (the owner's 2026-11-10 words gave it back the one word Autofill), held `CONTROL_SEEN_MS` as every main button; its click sends a plain `panel/fill`, which reaches that frame alone (`run/start`) with the tab granted the start of its next form (`sign-up.ts` `takeFill`: the same origin and application). The frame presses the Apply through the entry door (`press.ts` `pressEntry`, every guard of the agent's entry held: never beside a form, a shown challenge, a sign-in or a code; Workday's choice followed by its Apply Manually), then fills the form it brings, in this document (`formAfterWall`) or the next (the worker's grant). A wall or a choice wins over it; the page after submitting offers nothing. Since the review's L5 a press the panel offered that could not be made is told to the worker (`frame/wall`'s `entryRefused`, `FrameEntry.entryRefused`, `PageState.entryRefused`), and the panel says so in one line until the next press; never beside a wall or a choice, which say what is left themselves, and a hello does not keep it. **Since jr6** the button reads Autofill on every page of ours with no form, the frame reads the page again before it presses, the steps the site shows on the way, the refusals (one closed reason each) and the grant's following are as "jr6: Autofill on a posting: press, follow, fill" below says, which replaces this bullet where they differ.

### The Phase 2 review's fixes (2026-11-08)
What the review's fixes add, beyond the notes above that they change.
- **P2-stream** (`dce1fe3`; TailorIT `31296c8`):
  - **A field asked again in another batch** (H1): `RunAi` keeps what streamed by field and batch (`streamed`: key to batch hash), so a field whose answer streamed in one batch and which the frame then asks again in another (a free-text classification, then its draft) takes the second batch's stream, its wake-up and its failure, never the first batch's record. The backstop: a field planned again and asked anew more than `MAX_AI_REASKS` (4) times in one run (`loop.ts`) is given up, its row `llm-unavailable`, flagged (`content.ai-reask-limit`, no fields); each re-ask still counts as progress for the AI wait's cap. A normal chain asks anew two or three times at most.
  - **Held-back items** (M1): an item one round's answer task holds back (TailorIT's `classification.reason 'sensitive'`, or the extension's own answer screen for an item asked with its category) is classified first, by a classify task, as before one round (`RunAi.answers.classifyFirst`, `PlanAi.classifyFirst`): no answer is taken from it, and no `llm-disabled-for-category` classification is made up for it. Under `modes.ai 'all'` a legal or pay question naming its topic goes straight to the `all` scope and never reaches one round; one with no such topic, once classified `sensitive_legal`, is planned exactly as a classification first planned it before v5 (a test pins that parity).
  - **A streamed answer whose reply was then voided** (security 5): TailorIT voids a reply as a whole after its stream released items (a canary or AI talk anywhere in it, prose included; an item array that does not name each item once; two JSON blocks; the repair retry's `malformed`), and marks each released item `streamedThenVoided: true` in the finished result (answers and drafts; additive within API 6). An item the worker took from its own stream stands as written, and its key goes to the frame in `plan/ready`'s `reviews` (at most 500; `RunAi.takeReviews`); one it never took (its stream lost, the result polled) is not trusted (`llm-invalid-output`). The frame (`Orchestrator.aiReady(runId, reviews)`, `voidedAi`, `reviewedRows`) flags what the run wrote from such an answer or draft: the field stays as written, its row `flagged`, `needsUserAction`, reason `ai-reply-voided` (`REASONS`' copy), at rest or in the run's final rows; a draft still waiting for the person's confirmation and a blocked row stay as they are. A voided draft counts as an unconfirmed draft, so Submit mode Auto holds for it.
  - **The stream's limits** (security 6a): TailorIT's 429 (six streams per account, two per task) is taken as any stream failure other than 404: the worker polls the same task and streams the next batch again; only a 404 turns the stream off for the worker's life. **Line ends** (security 7a): `server-client.ts` holds a CR that ends a chunk (`carry`) until the next chunk shows whether an LF follows, so a CR LF split between two chunks stays one line end. TailorIT's side (security 7b, `stream.ts` `voidsReleased`; its router's stream counters; `queue.ts` `acquireFor`, background work never counted): a throwing `onText` is caught, the stream stops releasing and the task ends through its normal path.
  - **The mock** (L8b): one round's answer drops a category's answer by the request's scope, as TailorIT does (`sensitive_eeo` and `consent` under `all`, the standard list otherwise); and a task TailorIT waited for (`ai-paced` asked without respond-async) is answered as of its due time, so a timer firing a millisecond early never shows it `running` (a test with the clock frozen proves it).
- **P2-fill** (`d4c2904`):
  - **Hidden text in a rich-text box** (security 3): every plan and draft request and Edit with AI get only the box's visible reading (above). Edit with AI refuses a box whose `innerText` and visible reading differ beyond white space (off-screen, clipped, see-through or tiny text in it; `values.ts` `editableHidesText`; `content/orchestrator.ts` `rewrite`): nothing is sent, the box says why (`PAGE_COPY.aiEdit.outcomes.hidden`, a `RewriteOutcome` `'hidden'`; `content.ai-edit-refused {hidden}`), and the row is flagged `hidden-text-in-box` (a new reason, with its `REASONS` copy).
  - `greenhouse-bridge.browser.test.ts` loses a no-op filter and a stale comment (L8a); the rich-text fixture's header names the page patches' `dialogs.hold`.
- **P2-detect** (`8363026`, `a5ee006`, and the budget `6611481`):
  - **Oracle's own app, by its markup** (security 1): `dom/page-signals.ts` `oracleCandidateExperienceShown` requires the app's shell (`oj-dialog` and `minimal-dialog`, `ORACLE_SHELL`), its navigation (a skip-navigation or sitemap link) and its page markup (the apply flow's sections, blocks, header or main heading, or the job details' page), as all five Oracle fixtures hold them; `oracleShellShown` is the shell and navigation alone. The sentinel (`jobSignalOf`) says `platform` for an own-domain address only with that check, else the page goes on to the other signals, the form score among them (it reads a page again when it draws many nodes or a form, so a late-drawn app is recognised once drawn); `adaptersFor(url, doc)` gives an own-domain address Oracle's adapter (then the generic) only with it, else the site adapter alone (the content runtime passes its document); and the Oracle adapter checks again (`unprovenOwnDomain`, from the document's own address): without it, `submitButton`, `entryControl` and so `isFinalStep` answer null, null and 'unknown', so Submit mode Auto, the panel's Submit and Autofill's press of the site's Apply never happen there. The application key stays Oracle's by the address (`applicationOf`, the worker's `keyFitsFrame` and the frames' platform read no document): only the adapter differs, where the presses and claims live.
  - **The watcher after detect:** the content runtime sends `site/watch` once per document (`resolvePage`) when its page adapter is Oracle's and the worker injected it (`injectedHere`); `sites.watch` injects `SUBMIT_WATCH_FILE` in MAIN once per document only for an injected document of kind `site`, at an own-domain Oracle address by Chrome's sender URL (`platformOfUrl` 'oracle', no static match, `isCompanySiteUrl`), while the career sites act and never in a private tab (`unavailable` where Chrome refuses; `sites.injected {watch}`). A `fetch` reference the app took before the injection bypasses the wrapper: the late-install limit.
  - **Applied on an employer's own domain** (`content/runtime.ts` `arm`, `ownOracle`): after the network signs (`netSeen`), detection there also needs `oracleLeftForm`: the app's shell still there, the adapter's Submit the press saw no longer shown, and no refusal (`refusalNow` 0), read again at each change until the watch ends. Oracle draws no completion message (Jobright's Oracle rule is network-only), so "the form left, the app still there" is the document's part, from the fixtures' markup alone; a refused Submit keeps the form and shows its errors (`hjf-after-submit`). On Oracle's own hosts the signs are read alone, as before. A sign missed ends as the existing "unconfirmed" press notice, and the person marks the job applied.
  - **`platformOfUrl`'s other callers** (L3; `shared/hosts.ts` `staticPlatformOfUrl`, the platform only where a static match runs): `launcher.ts` `siteKeyOf` keys an own domain by its registrable domain's hash, per employer, never 'oracle'; `agent.ts` `agentAddressOf` never opens an own-domain Oracle address (TailorIT's `agentApplicationUrl` gives none today: the guard if it ever does); `platform/chrome.ts` `atsPageAt` and `onAtsHost` leave own domains to `reports.pageNow`'s company-site branch, so the panel offers a reload there only while the career sites act, and the worker's on-load look-up (`onLoaded`) reads the static hosts only.
  - **Passive detection** (security 7d, docs only): since the watcher wraps `fetch` and `XMLHttpRequest` at `document_start` on its candidate pages, the job lists included since M3, any script there can tell the extension is installed without a fill or a click. PLAN decision 14 accepts it, as Jobright's own wrappers do, and M3 needs the early install.

### Messages, logs and copy
- **Message catalogue:** `page/patches` and (since the review) `site/watch` (content → worker), and `plan/ready` (worker → content; since the review with `reviews`), in the tables below; `submit/detected`'s evidence gains `'network'`; `frame/wall` gains `entry` and, since the review, `entryRefused`, and `PageState` the same two (a posting's Apply; since jr6 also `entering` and `refusal`, A0's); the page patches' ops gain `dialogs.hold-alerts`.
- **Logs** (`shared/log.ts`, their `v5 p2-fill` and `v5 p2-stream` blocks; counts and flags only, never a key, an item or a value): `content.dialogs-held {alerts, confirms}`, `content.picker {input, fileSystem, wrote}`, `content.resume-parse {ms, changed, parsed}`, `patches.injected`, `patches.refused {form | failed}`, `content.wall`'s `entry` (a posting's Apply pressed had a code of its own until jr6, whose `content.entry` and `content.entry-step` say how an entry went); `server.stream-ended` (status, item count, ms, whether with its task, why as one flag), `ai.stream-unsupported`, `ai.stream-differs {items}`, `ai.one-round-unsupported`, and `content.ai-waited`'s `woken`; since the review `content.ai-reask-limit`, `content.ai-edit-refused`'s `hidden` and `sites.injected`'s `watch`.
- **Copy:** the posting button's name and its "?", and since the review its refusal line, drafts for the owner that jr6 replaced (`COPY.panel.fill`, `COPY.mainView.entryHelp`, `entering`, `entryRefused`: "jr6" below); since the review the `dialogs` toast's words, `aiEdit.outcomes.hidden`, and the reasons `hidden-text-in-box` and `ai-reply-voided` (`REASONS`), drafts too; the disclosure (below).

### Disclosure version 11 (`shared/stored.ts` `DISCLOSURE_VERSION`, `COPY.options.connection.disclosure`)
docs/PRIVACY.md holds it word for word (`src/shared/copy.test.ts`). New: on Workday's, ClearCompany's, Oracle Recruiting Cloud's (Oracle's own, or an employer's once Oracle's application form shows there) and SuccessFactors' candidate pages a small script of the extension's runs inside the page to see the site's own send request, its only output a yes or no, read on Workday, Oracle Recruiting Cloud and SuccessFactors from the request or its answer, counted only after a Submit in that page; while a fill runs, another small script in the page holds back the alerts its writes or an upload's wait bring and the confirms its own typing or choosing brings (never a confirm asked during a wait, never one after the person's own click or key), the person then shown how many on the page (unless they set its notifications to Never show them), and may answer the site's own file picker with the chosen resume for that one upload, only to that button's own file input or the picker its press opens, the site's scripts able to tell either is there; rich-text boxes written with the browser's own editing; Open Application pressing a posting's own Apply when the person chooses it; and the AI's answers streamed back from TailorIT as each is ready. A connected browser shows it again with I understand (`auth.ts` `disclosed` reads the stored version against `DISCLOSURE_VERSION`), and until then what it governs stays off as before. The Phase 2 review changed its dialogs, picker and Oracle wording without a new version: version 11 had reached no one yet.

### check-build, lint and sizes (`scripts/check-build.mjs`, its `// v5 p2-detect` and `// v5 p2-fill` blocks)
- **Three content scripts:** ours, the bridge (world MAIN) and the submit watcher (world MAIN, `content-scripts/submit-watch.js`, told from the bridge by its file). The watcher: exactly `SUBMIT_WATCH_MATCHES`, all frames, `document_start`, no CSS or frame-widening keys, within `SUBMIT_WATCH_BUDGET` (30 KiB: 22,024 bytes when added, about 3 KiB its own and the rest the bridge's address decision it shares; 28,571 at `983c81e` with Oracle's and SuccessFactors' signs, Workday's and Oracle's body facts and Oracle's own-domain reading, under 28 KiB; 29,146 at `be29014` with the review's own install decision, `submitWatchInstallsAt`, so 30 KiB since `6611481`); its bundle one anonymous function naming none of `SUBMIT_WATCH_NEVER_NAMES` (the extension's APIs, `postMessage`, `.json(`, `.arrayBuffer(`, `.blob(`, `.formData(`, `getReader`, `responseXML`, every header reader, storage, `document.cookie`; since the follow-up `.clone(`, `.text(`, `.body` and `responseText` are allowed, the reason beside it). Run in a bare vm it adds no global and no listener, wraps fetch, open and send exactly at `SUBMIT_WATCH_INSTALLS_AT` (Workday, ClearCompany, Oracle; since the review their candidate apps' job lists included, M3, and an own-domain Oracle list, the bundle's own decision, which the manifest never runs there), SuccessFactors' `applyJob` exactly at `SUBMIT_WATCH_PROXY_AT`, nothing at `SUBMIT_WATCH_SILENT_AT` (since the review also myworkdaysite's tenant-only and sign-in paths, ClearCompany's `/hr/home` and an own-domain address under another prefix); and `SUBMIT_WATCH_DRIVES` drives it with answers holding a person's details beside the fact: ClearCompany's bodies are never touched, and nothing is dispatched but a sign's own detail.
- **No static content script on an HR system** (since the review's security 2): none of ours, the bridge or the watcher may match an address of `HR_ADDRESSES` (now holding `n12.ultipro.com/<T>/JobBoard/...` and `n12.ultipro.ca/<T>/JobBoard/...`), and `patternMatches` reads a `*` anywhere in a pattern's path, as Chrome does. One address is skipped, with its comment: `app.careerplug.com/jobs/`, under the `*.careerplug.com/jobs/*` match, which Chrome's patterns cannot narrow and `hosts.ts` refuses as no platform's.
- **The page patches** (`checkPagePatches`): `page-patches.js` at each build's root, listed nowhere in the manifest (no content script, no web-accessible resource), within `PAGE_PATCHES_BUDGET` 8 KiB (4,825 bytes when added, 5,109 since the review), one anonymous function called at once, naming none of `chrome.`, `browser.`, `postMessage`, `fetch`, `XMLHttpRequest`, `localStorage`, `sessionStorage`, `indexedDB`, `sendBeacon` nor WXT's content-script context; run in a bare context it throws nothing, adds no global, listens for `tailorit:patch` alone (capture) and leaves the page's `alert`, `confirm`, `showOpenFilePicker` and `HTMLInputElement.prototype.click` as they were.
- **Lint** (`eslint.config.mjs`): a page-world script under `src/page/` imports only its protocol (`../dom/bridge-protocol`, `../dom/submit-net-protocol` or `../dom/patch-protocol`), and `./dialogs` and `./picker` beside the patches.
- **Budgets and sizes** (production, on the tree merged at `788bf31`): the bridge 26 KiB (`BRIDGE_BUDGET`: 20,939 bytes at `019f489`, 26,507 with the parsers' new pages and variants), the submit watcher 28,571 (then 28 KiB), the page patches 4,825 (8 KiB, new), the sentinel 19 KiB (`SENTINEL_BUDGET`: 17,529 → 18,657 for the variants' patterns it rules out and Oracle's own-domain recognition), the content script 842,341 bytes (845,149 in the e2e variant; 1 MiB), the sidebar's bundle 754,276 (800 KiB). After the review (its final verify's builds): the bridge 26,593 bytes (26 KiB, 31 to spare), the submit watcher 29,146 (30 KiB), the page patches 5,109, the sentinel 19,112 (19 KiB).
