  - After review the branch was fast-forwarded into the owner's checkout (`v1.2`, `65dc16e`, 2026-10-31), with their dev server stopped, and the worktree was retired. The scratch data folder stays.
- **The contract between them:** owned by TailorIT (`packages/shared/src/autofill/`, zod, `apiVersion: 3` since 2026-11-02; 1 at first), and copied byte for byte into the extension's `src/contract/tailorit/` by `npm run contract:pull -- F:\Develop\TailorIT`. Both repos test the copy against the same hashes and fixtures, so the two sides cannot drift silently.
- **Design reports:** the three reports written during planning (pure core, runtime, TailorIT's side) are saved in Phase 0 as `docs/design/{core,runtime,tailorit}.md`. They hold the full tables: rules, patterns, golden cases, DDL, routes. Where a report and this plan differ, the plan wins. They were corrected to agree with this file when they were saved.

## How it works at run time
```
ATS frame (top or embedded iframe)          extension service worker                  TailorIT (http://localhost:3100)
┌───────────────────────────────────────┐   ┌─────────────────────────────────────┐   ┌──────────────────────────────┐
│ content script (isolated world)       │   │ pure core: resolver, matcher, the   │   │ /api/autofill/* (token only) │
│  adapter, scanner, DOM Guard checks   │──►│  Guard's pure checks, planners      │──►│  profile, sensitive part,    │
│   (visibility, camouflage, tripwire)  │◄──│ profile, saved answers, vocabularies│◄──│  lookup, resume file,        │
│  filler + read-back, reactive loop,   │   │  (memory); token (IndexedDB);       │   │  AI pick/classify/draft      │
│  step machine; launcher, notices,     │   │  settings, RunState (storage)       │   │  (Claude CLI), saved answers │
│  in-page panel                        │   │ resume bytes → chunked base64       │   │                              │
└───────────────────────────────────────┘   └─────────────────────────────────────┘   └──────────────────────────────┘
    FieldSpec[] ──►   ◄── PlannedWrite[] (values for this frame's fields only)
    reports (no values) ──► side panel or in-page panel (review) ◄── the user
```
- **What the content script does:**
  - It scans its frame into `FieldSpec[]`: visible fields only, every label and option through `visibleText`.
  - It runs the DOM Guard checks, fills and reads back, settles, and runs the step machine's reducer (`reduceRun`).
  - It keeps every canary in memory for its own pre-write scan.
  - Its provenance re-check before each write is structural: the origin is present, the path pattern valid, the rule known, and an `llm-pick`'s option text unchanged.
- **What the worker does:**
  - It holds the profile and the saved answers, both fetched at `run/begin` and dropped together, plus the vocabularies (about 2 MB, parsed once).
  - It resolves and matches, runs the Guard's pure checks (including whether a profile path resolves), and answers `PlannedWrite[]`. So a frame never holds the whole profile, only the values for its own fields.
  - It submits the AI tasks.
  - It decides which canaries may be stored: its `runstate/put` handler drops every canary that equals a profile value. If the profile is not loaded at that moment, it loads it first; if that fails, it stores an empty `canarySet`. It never stores a canary it has not checked.
  - It enforces the RunState rules when storing, and derives the opt-in counters from report rows.
- **No install secret:**
  - An `llm-pick`'s option check uses an unkeyed SHA-256 of the clean option text as sent (page text, never stored).
  - Flip detection hashes values with a random per-run key held in memory only.
- **Starting a fill:** one click on the launcher or in the side panel fills the frame's form. The launcher accepts trusted clicks only (`event.isTrusted`), so a page can never start a fill. (Since v5 Phase 1 the top frame's launcher, a pill, opens the panel and its Autofill fills; an embedded frame's corner launcher still fills on its click.)
- **Isolation:** the page cannot message the worker or start a fill. Nothing of the extension announces itself to a page: the content script is an unlisted script with no WXT content-script context, whose start-up event any page could listen for (the v3 Phase 6/7b review's round 2, E1). The forged WXT events (trap 11) are listened for only where the bridge installs, decided once, as the bridge's is, from the address the document was loaded at (`runtime.ts` `loadedHref`: its navigation entry, which no pushState of the page's moves), so elsewhere on an ATS domain no listener keyed to the extension's id runs for a page to time, nor does the page's own route to a posting address add one (round 3, X1, and round 4, Y1: following the route let a page push a posting address, time the listener and push back; e2e `00-smoke`, the debugger's listing of every world's listeners, after a pushState and with one made before the content script started). The content script says `frame/hello` only where the frame's address reads as a posting or its application form on its platform's candidate host (or the page after submitting: `src/adapters/address.ts`), so the worker never hears of an employer's own pages on the ATS domains the manifest matches (S3). The page-world bridge installs at document_start on any page of a host that serves only public job postings (`job-boards.greenhouse.io`, `boards.greenhouse.io`, `jobs.lever.co`, `jobs.eu.lever.co`, `jobs.ashbyhq.com`, `jobs.smartrecruiters.com`, `apply.workable.com`, `<tenant>[.wd<n>].myworkdayjobs.com`, and the new ATSs' candidate-only hosts: `jobs.jobvite.com`, `ats.rippling.com`, `recruiting.ultipro.com`, `jobs.dayforcehcm.com`, `<tenant>.careers.hibob.com`), so a board's client-side route to a posting keeps it (round 2, E9); on the domains that also hold an employer's own pages (iCIMS, Taleo, SuccessFactors, Oracle Cloud and its government cloud, Workday's shared `myworkdaysite.com`, `jobs.workable.com` and the new ATSs' path-scoped hosts) only where its address at document_start is a posting or its form, and nowhere else, not even as a silent listener a page could probe after a pushState to a posting-looking address (`bridgeInstallsAt`). There is no web-accessible resource but the preview page (since v1.5, at its dynamic address alone, showing a page that loads it nothing without the dialog's grant: "v1.5"), every message is zod-checked, and `externally_connectable` is limited to TailorIT's origin, for pairing only.
  - **The one page-world script** is the bridge (docs/SPEC-AMENDMENTS.md §12, the owner's "React first, like Jobright"): `src/entrypoints/page-bridge.content.ts`, a second content script with `world: 'MAIN'`, the same ATS hosts, every frame, from `document_start`, about 21 KB (20,921 bytes) with the shared domains' address parsers it installs by (`src/adapters/bridge-address.ts` `bridgeInstallsAt`, re-exported by `address.ts`: any page of a public job host, a posting or its form on a shared domain, read by `SHARED_DOMAIN_PARSERS` alone, the v3 Phase 6/7b review's S3 and its round 2's E9; check-build holds it to 24 KiB ("The page-world bridge's size" above) and reads its bundle: one anonymous function, naming no `chrome.`, `browser.` or `postMessage`, adding no global and listening for its command alone, and only there). It runs `src/page/bridge.ts`, which imports only its protocol (`src/dom/bridge-protocol.ts`; lint, which also forbids it the extension's and the page's globals, and `test/entrypoints.test.ts` keep it so): no zod, no React, no messaging, no storage, no browser API, no global. It holds no data and keeps no state but one capture listener on the document.
  - **The page fully controls the main world:** it can read every command, wrap every function the bridge calls, and forge commands and answers. So nothing may be sent there that the page should not read; later ops send no profile text there beyond what is being written into that same field at that moment. A page can also tell the extension is installed, from `document_start`, by the bridge's acknowledgement of a command.
  - **The protocol:** the content script (`src/dom/bridge.ts`) dispatches a non-bubbling, cancelable, composed `tailorit:bridge` CustomEvent on the element it is about, its detail a JSON string (a string crosses Chrome's worlds; an object does not): `{v: 1, id, op, ...}`, with an op of the list, a random id (`crypto.getRandomValues`: `randomUUID` needs a secure context, which an https form inside an http page is not), a label of at most 300 code points and no other key, read strictly on both sides. The bridge acts on the event's composed path's first element (inside an open shadow root too), an element of its own document, acknowledges the command with `preventDefault()` and answers it with `tailorit:bridge-result` on the same element, `{v: 1, id, ok, reason}`, both inside the dispatch. A command nobody acknowledged (no bridge in the frame), and one acknowledged but unanswered when the dispatch returns (something on the page took it), get no answer at once: nothing is waited for. Nothing there can end a run: any throw is no answer.
  - **The ops:** `react-select.choose {label, multi}`, react-select's own `selectOption` for the one option whose label, as react-select reads it (its `getOptionLabel`), is exactly the label sent (white space collapsed; groups flattened; none or two, a disabled option or select, the wrong kind, and an option chosen already are refused); and `react.field`, the nearest React component's field (`props.field`, else `props.fieldEntry.field`) as `{type, path}`, two printable strings of at most 100 characters, for Ashby's adapter (Phase 2).
  - **What crosses:** into the page's world, the op, whether the select is a multi-select, a random id and the chosen option's clean label as the frame read it from the page, which reveals the answer as the fill will, a moment earlier; never a profile value. Back, a reason from a closed list and those two strings. A forged command can only make the bridge choose a page's own widget's own option; a forged answer is never trusted: the content script reads the widget back itself and falls back to its events (Filler, "React first"). After the first command nothing answered, or the first choice it claimed that never read back, the run's bridge is off (`BridgeRun`).
- **Messages** (`src/messaging`; the full catalogue goes in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`):
  - **Envelope.** Every message is `{v: 1, type, …}`, parsed by a strict zod union per channel, with every string and array capped. Every answer is `{ok: true, data}` or `{ok: false, error}`, where `error` is a closed list.
  - **The worker checks each sender:**
    - content messages need our own extension id, a tab and a frame, an ATS address and an active document;
    - page messages must come from our own extension pages;
    - external messages need TailorIT's exact origin and the pending pairing `state`.
    - A known type on the wrong channel is refused.
  - **Content to worker:**
    - `frame/hello` and `run/begin`, which answers a run id and the settings, never the profile;
    - `plan/request`: the frame's whole scan as `FieldSpec[]` (at most 500), the keys this pass plans (`planKeys`, a subset of the specs' keys), and the page's canaries (at most 32) and notices. It answers `PlannedWrite[]`, value-free rows and the keys waiting for the LLM, for `planKeys` only;
    - `plan/poll`, which returns those keys' writes once the AI task is done;
    - `runstate/*`, `report/put` and `policy/hit` (a posting page's value-free pattern id);
    - `panel/open`, from the launcher's trusted click;
    - the `resume` port.
  - **Worker to content:**
    - start, continue (after a pause) and cancel;
    - a policy card's decision, to the one frame that waits at it (since v4 none waits, and the frame answers `expired`);
    - focus a field, confirm a draft;
    - `field/describe`: the labels of a row's suggested options, fetched only when the row is expanded, never stored;
    - `report/update`: the tab's combined report, sent to the frame that shows the in-page panel while it is open;
    - read an answer the user asked to save: it is sent straight to TailorIT and never stored.
  - **External:** `pair/complete {state, code}` only.
  - **The worker's limits:**
    - It calls only `<origin>/api/autofill/*` and `GET <origin>/api/ai/tasks/<uuid>`, with `credentials: 'omit'`, `redirect: 'error'` and `cache: 'no-store'`. It never cancels (`DELETE`s) an AI task: TailorIT gives callers that ask the same one task, and prunes every task itself (the Phase 4 review, T-F2).
    - Every call, the resume included, has `AbortSignal.timeout(25_000)`, because Chrome may stop a worker whose fetch waits longer than 30 s.
    - AI routes answer within 10 s, or return a task to poll every 1.5 s, with a 120 s cap.
    - After a worker restart, `plan/poll` for an unknown run answers `expired`, and the content script re-sends `plan/request` for its waiting fields.
    - A resubmitted batch reattaches through `dedupeKey` while the task is queued or running. A finished one is re-read by the task id kept in RunState (an id, never a value), within its one-hour retention.
  - **Resume transfer:**
    - The content script asks only when a planned write targets a file field.
    - **Before any fetch:** a legacy `.doc` (`fileKind: doc`) is refused: "Your resume is an old Word file. Choose a PDF resume in TailorIT."
    - **What the worker accepts:**
      - files up to 10 MiB, so at most 40 chunks;
      - a PDF whose bytes start with `%PDF-`;
      - a DOCX with the ZIP signature and `X-TailorIT-File-Kind: docx`. It goes only into an input with no `accept`, or one that allows `.docx` or its MIME type; otherwise the field is flagged `pdf-only-input`.
    - **Sending:** a header (name, type, size, SHA-256 checked against `X-TailorIT-Sha256`), then 256 KiB base64 chunks, each acknowledged before the next.
    - **Receiving:** the content script decodes into one buffer, checks size and hash, builds the `File`, and retries once from scratch on any mismatch (then `resume-transfer-failed`). A failed or timed-out fetch flags the field `resume-unavailable`.
    - Both sides release the bytes on completion or within 60 s.
- **Purity is enforced four ways:**
  - `tsconfig.core.json` (lib ES2023, `types: []`): `document`, `URL`, `crypto` and `chrome` cannot even be typed.
  - An ESLint override for `src/core/**` bans impure globals, `Date.now`, `Math.random`, dynamic import, zod, and imports of impure layers, with inline disables refused. Only `import type` from `src/contract/tailorit` is allowed, since it is erased at build.
  - WXT's auto-imports are off.
  - A Vitest test loads every core module in Node with no DOM.
  - Time, randomness, vocabularies and the LLM come in as parameters.
- **Storage:**
  - Content scripts get no storage at all: the platform wrapper hands them none.
  - The worker also calls `storage.local.setAccessLevel({accessLevel: 'TRUSTED_CONTEXTS'})` in `onInstalled` and at every start, before it handles any content message. This needs Chrome 140+, hence the 144 floor. A rejection blocks filling and is shown on the options page.

  | Data | Where | How long |
  |---|---|---|
  | The bearer token | the extension origin's IndexedDB (a content script cannot open it), plus the worker's memory | until disconnect, expiry or a 401 |
  | The profile | the worker's memory | the run; dropped after 5 idle minutes. The sensitive part (authorization, EEO, age, pronouns, pay) is fetched only when the worker meets such a question |
  | Saved answers | the worker's memory, fetched with the profile | the run |
  | Resume bytes, and (Phase G) a cover letter's | the worker, then the content script | seconds |
  | The run's cover letter (Phase G): TailorIT's id of it, where it stands, whether its AI wrote it (TailorIT's word, or written during the run), and the lookup's job id it was asked with | the worker's memory | until the run is replaced or forgotten, the token changes, or the worker stops |
  | Settings | `storage.local` | until changed |
  | RunState (no values) | `storage.local`, indexed per tab in `storage.session` | 24 hours after its last update; at most 20 kept, each at most 256 KiB (`MAX_RUN_STATE_BYTES`). Its records hold at most `RUN_STATE_RECORD_CAPS` entries |
  | Reports (no values) | `storage.session` | until the tab closes, the document that sent it is gone (its frame loads another, or the worker finds it gone), or the browser restarts |
  | Registered frames (origin, platform, document id, flags; never an address) | `storage.session`, `frames:<tabId>:<frameId>` | until the tab closes, the worker finds the frame's document gone (`frame/ping`), or the browser restarts |
  | Runs (no values): tab, frame, document id, application key, dry run, and what became of the run's one sensitive fetch | `storage.session`, `runs:<runId>` | until the tab closes, the frame begins another run, the document it was begun in is gone, or the browser restarts; at most 50 kept |
  | Policy hits from a posting page: pattern ids, and whether a posting page was read and clear (`{hits, clear}`) | `storage.session`, `policy:<applicationKey>`, their order (keys only) in `policy-order` | until the browser restarts; at most 200 applications, the least recently recorded or read going first |
  | The pending pairing: a single-use state of 32 random bytes | `storage.session`, `pairing` | 10 minutes, or its first use |
  | The account's name and email, from TailorIT's session | the worker's memory | until the token changes or the worker stops |
  | Each run's lookup answer: the job, its stored posting text (read only by the policy patterns), the suggested resume | the worker's memory | until the run is replaced or forgotten, or the worker stops |
  | Accepted matches (sealed: the key's value part and the chosen option's normalized label as HMAC-SHA-256 under a secret of the browser session's, kept beside them; the v3 Phase 7a review) | `storage.session`, in memory, never on disk. Never unconfirmed LLM picks; never EEO, legal, consent or sensitive fields | until the browser restarts |
  | Anonymous counters | `storage.local`, only when opted in | until cleared |

## Message catalogue
Every shape is a strict zod schema in `src/messaging/schemas.ts` (since snapshot round 2 the extension pages', the ports' and TailorIT's connect page's in `src/messaging/page-schemas.ts`, which the content script never imports); this section says who sends each message, what the worker does with it, and what it answers. Built in Phase 2 (`src/background`).

**The envelope.**
- A message is `{v: 1, type, …}`. An answer is `{ok: true, data}` or `{ok: false, error, retryAfterSeconds?}`, where `error` is one of `bad_message`, `bad_sender`, `not_connected`, `not_implemented`, `unavailable`, `rate_limited`, `too_large`, `expired`, `internal` and (the v4 review) `not_found` ("v4", `signin/credential` refusals).
- In every channel: an unknown type, an extra field or a value past its cap is `bad_message`; a known type on the wrong channel is `bad_sender`; a handler that throws is `internal`.
- Receivers parse answers too. Each answer's data schema is named below, and `CONTENT_ANSWER_SCHEMAS`, `PAGE_ANSWER_SCHEMAS`, `FRAME_ANSWER_SCHEMAS` and `EXTERNAL_ANSWER_SCHEMAS` map every type to it (wrap one in `answerSchema`).

**Content script → worker** (`runtime.sendMessage`).
- The sender needs this extension's id, a tab, a frame, an ATS address (`shared/hosts.ts`) and an active document (since v3 Phase 8, or a document the worker injected; since v5 Phase 1 a tab's top frame may also send the sidebar's page messages and open its ports: "v5 Phase 1", "The worker").
- Every message but `ping` and `panel/open` waits until Chrome has answered the storage access level.

| Message | Payload | Answer (`data`) | Refusals |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ping` | none | `{pong: true}` (`PingAnswerSchema`) | none |
| `frame/hello` | `isTop`, `hasForm` | `{frameId, connected, settings}` (`FrameHelloAnswerSchema`): `connected` is whether a token is held; `settings` holds panel placement, auto-advance, the AI switches and (Phase 6) the offer to save answers | none |
| `run/begin` | `applicationKey`, `trigger` (`launcher`, `panel` or `resume`), `dryRun` | `{runId, settings, policy: {postingPage, storedCopy}}` (`RunBeginAnswerSchema`), never the profile or the lookup | `not_connected`. `unavailable`: TailorIT could not be read, or Chrome refused the access level. `expired`: `resume` without this tab's unfinished RunState. `rate_limited`: 6 a minute per tab. `bad_message`: the key is not `<platform>:<hex16>` with the frame host's platform |
| `plan/request` | `runId`, `pass`, `specs` (the frame's whole scan, at most 500: the context the planner reads across the frame), `planKeys` (the keys this pass plans, at most 500, each one a spec's key), `canaries` (at most 32), `notices`; optional `repeatables` (Phase 5: the repeatable sections the frame's adapter shows on the step, each kind once, at most 3: `{kind, entries}`, `entries` the frame's own block→entry assignment, at most `MAX_REPEATABLE_BLOCKS` (100) indices or nulls, or null for the worker to assign); optional `postingText` (Phase 6: the top frame's visible text outside the form root, at most `MAX_POSTING_TEXT` (20,000) code points, sent while drafts are on and never in a dry run: a draft's job text); optional `revealEdges` (the Phase 6 review: the loop's reveal edges on the step, `[parent, child]` base keys, at most 100, for a draft's context); optional `posting` (Phase G: `{company, title}` the frame's page title names, each at most 200, sent with a pass that plans an upload: a cover letter for a posting TailorIT does not have) | `{writes, results, awaiting, persistableCanaries, repeatables?, savable?}` (`PlanAnswerSchema`), for `planKeys` only: the other specs get no row and no write. `savable` (the Phase 6 review): the planned keys whose answer the person may save; the frame offers Save my answer on those only. `repeatables`, one per section named: `{kind, profile, entries, current}`, the profile's entry count, each block's entry (null: none) and whether that entry is a current job; counts, indices and flags only | `bad_message`: a planKey that is not among the specs, a section named twice, an unknown kind, an assignment past its cap. `expired`: a run the worker does not know, or another frame's or document's. `not_connected`, `unavailable`, `internal` |
| `plan/poll` | `runId`, `keys` | the same shape (Phase 4): the writes and rows of those keys whose LLM answers are in, planned again over the scan they were asked from; the keys still waited for in `awaiting`; a key the worker does not know left out | `expired`: the run is unknown, or the worker knows none of the keys (it restarted): the frame plans them again with `plan/request` |
| `draft/rewrite` (the owner's amendments) | `runId`, `fieldKey` (a spec's key), `specs`, `canaries`; optional `postingText`, `revealEdges`: Edit with AI, on the person's trusted click while the run rests | as `plan/request`, for that one field: it awaits its draft (asked with the box's current text), which `plan/poll` brings. Plan answers also carry `aiEditable` (the keys the page may offer Edit with AI on) and `aiAttestation` (the scan holds an "I did not use AI" box); `plan/request` carries `noAi` for a profile-only run | `bad_message`: a `fieldKey` that is no spec's, or a dry run. `expired`, `not_connected`, `unavailable`, `internal` |
| `runstate/get` | `applicationKey` | `{runState}` (`RunStateGetAnswerSchema`): null when there is none, it expired, or another tab holds it | `bad_message` |
| `runstate/put` | `runState` | `{updatedAt}` (`RunStatePutAnswerSchema`), the worker's own clock, which the stored entry carries | `bad_message`: a key or platform that is not the frame's, or a record past its cap (`RUN_STATE_RECORD_CAPS`, below). `too_large`: its stored form is over `MAX_RUN_STATE_BYTES` (256 KiB); nothing is read or written |
| `runstate/clear` | `applicationKey` | `{}` (`DoneAnswerSchema`) | `bad_message` |
| `report/put` | `runId`, `report` (`FrameReport`) | `{}` | `expired`. `bad_message`: the report names another run or frame. `rate_limited`: 4 a second per frame |
| `policy/hit` | `applicationKey`, `patternId` (null: the posting page was read and is clear) | `{}` | `rate_limited`: 10 in 10 s per frame, counted before anything else. `bad_message`: an id that is not `[a-z0-9.-]{1,40}`, or a key that is not the frame's |
| `panel/open` | none: the launcher's trusted click under Chrome's side panel; since the v5 Phase 1 review (S6, S7) also the top frame's, on the person's trusted, seen click on a sidebar link to `sidepanel.html` (a preview, the sensitive answers, I understand, an Auto choice), sent inside the click | `{}`, once the side panel opened for the sender's tab | `unavailable`: the browser refused (the toolbar button still opens it) |
| `panel/shown` (Phase 6) | `open`: the in-page panel opened (`true`) or closed (`false`) in this frame, on the person's trusted click | `{}` (`DoneAnswerSchema`). Open: the worker keeps the frame as the tab's panel frame (`panel-frame:<tabId>`: its frame id and document) and, with the in-page panel chosen, sends it the tab's reports at once (`report/update`). Closed: the record goes when it is this frame's | `rate_limited`: `PANEL_SHOWN_LIMIT` (10 in 10 s) per frame. `bad_message`: anything but `open` |
| `answer/save` (Phase 6, wave 2) | `fieldKey`: the in-page panel's Save my answer, on the person's trusted click, for one of this frame's own fields | `{outcome}` (`SaveAnswerAnswerSchema`), as `panel/saveAnswer` for the sending frame (below) | as `panel/saveAnswer`; `expired` also when the frame is not the registered document |
| `job/lookup`, `job/add` (v2) | none: the frame's answer to the worker's `job/refresh {action}` (below). The worker looks the sender's own address up (`POST /lookup`, `includePostingText: false`, the tab's chosen resume) or adds its posting (`POST /jobs`, only for an address `addablePosting` reads), keeps the result as that document's job card, and answers | `{}` (`DoneAnswerSchema`): never what TailorIT said, so no card data enters the page's process | `expired`: no `job/refresh` of that action waits for this very frame and document (a page cannot make the worker ask TailorIT). `rate_limited`: `JOB_RELAY_LIMIT` (6 a minute) per frame |
| `lists/known` (snapshot round 2) | optional `stored`: the ids of the stored lists the scan names that the run has not asked for (`STORED_LIST_IDS`, at most 6). The site is Chrome's sender address, never a payload. Sent before a step's loop when its scan holds a learnable dropdown (once a run) or a stored list not asked for yet | `{lists: [{key, labels}], stored?: [{id, labels}]}` (`LearnedListsAnswerSchema`): the site's learned lists, the most recently learned of each label key, at most `MAX_LEARNED_PER_HOST` (60), and the stored lists asked for (`background/stored-lists.ts`) | `rate_limited`: with `lists/learn`, `LEARNED_LISTS_LIMIT` (60 a minute) per tab |
| `lists/learn` (snapshot round 2) | `key` (16 hex digits: the label slug's, with the value its list depends on: `learnedListKey`), `labels` (1 to 300, each 1 to 160 characters: page text, never a value) | `{}` (`DoneAnswerSchema`); nothing kept from a private tab (`lists.private` logged) | as `lists/known` |
| `sidebar/load` (v5 Phase 1) | none: the top frame opening the sidebar, or an embedded frame opening its in-page card. The worker runs `sidebar-app.js` in that one document (`scripting.executeScript`) | `{tabId}` (`SidebarLoadAnswerSchema`): the sender's tab, which every message of the sidebar names | `bad_sender`: the sentinel. `expired`: "Where the panel shows" is Chrome's side panel. `rate_limited`: `SIDEBAR_LOAD_LIMIT` (20 a minute) per tab. `unavailable`: Chrome refused |
| `sidebar/options` (v5 Phase 1) | optional `section` (`^[a-z][a-z-]{0,39}$`): the sidebar's link to the options page, which a page may not open | `{}` (`DoneAnswerSchema`) once `options.html#section` opened in a new tab | `bad_sender`: any frame but frame 0, or the sentinel; since jr6 the tab's claim frame (an iCIMS portal's), in its registered document, is taken too, for `section` `settings-signup` alone (the no-sign-in toast's Open; `index.ts`). `rate_limited`: with the launcher's messages, `LAUNCHER_LIMIT` (30 a minute) per tab |
| `page/patches` (v5 Phase 2) | none: the first run that writes begins in this document (never a dry run). The worker runs `page-patches.js` in that one document, world MAIN (`scripting.executeScript` with its document id) | `{}` (`DoneAnswerSchema`) once Chrome ran it | `bad_sender`: the sentinel, or a sender with no document id. `expired`: the frame's registry entry is for another document or holds no form. `rate_limited`: `PAGE_PATCHES_LIMIT` (20 a minute) per frame. `unavailable`: Chrome refused (the writes go on without the patches) |
| `site/watch` (v5 Phase 2 review) | none: an injected company page whose page adapter is Oracle's (the app's markup shown and its application form found), once per document | `{}` (`DoneAnswerSchema`) once the worker ran `content-scripts/submit-watch.js` in that document, world MAIN, once per document | `bad_sender`: not a document the worker injected as a company page (kind `site`), a private tab, or no document id. `bad_message`: Chrome's address for the sender is not Oracle's Candidate Experience on an employer's own domain (`platformOfUrl` 'oracle', no static match, `isCompanySiteUrl`). `unavailable`: Company career sites do not act, or Chrome refused |
| `entry/pressed` (jr6, A0; F3a's handler) | optional `nextKey` (an application key, the press's same-origin destination as an adapter reads it): the frame that pressed a site's Apply or a step on the way, at each press of the person's entry (`runtime.ts enter`; never the agent's, which sends `agent/entered`) | `{}` (`DoneAnswerSchema`) once the tab's entry grant is armed (`sign-up.ts pressed`): a pending grant takes its first press, an armed one is armed again and keeps the key | `rate_limited`: 6 a minute per tab (`ENTRY_PRESSED_LIMIT`), counted first. `bad_sender`: not the tab's application frame (`wallFrameOf`), not the document registered for it, or Chrome's address off that frame's origin. `expired`: no live fill grant for the tab, a frame off the grant's origin, a document the grant did not reach, or neither the grant nor the press names an application key (a pending entry grant then ends). Any live fill grant is armed, one with no entry yet included (`grant.entry ?? {…}`: Workday's choice page, where the person's Autofill pressed a declared choice). Never a page address or a word of the page |
| `chain/facts {facts}` (jr6, SHELL-B, ICIMS-17) | `facts`: `ChainStepFactsSchema` (`stepId` matching `^Step_[A-Za-z]{1,48}$`, `lastStep`, `packet` `{n, m}` or null, `counters`; an id, a boolean and counts) or null: the tab's claim frame, a portal page that frames an iCIMS IForm leaf, saying what its step bar says, after its hello and at each change of its page (`content/chain-frame.ts` `watchChainFacts`) | `{}` (`DoneAnswerSchema`) once kept for the sender's document (`storage.session` `chainfacts:<tabId>:<frameId>`) and handed to the leaf frames chained from it (`chain/leafFacts`) | `expired`: a private tab, or not the tab's claim frame on a portal whose pages frame chain pages, in its registered document (`chain.ts` `factsSaid`, `factsPortalOf`) |

- **What the worker does with each:**
  - `frame/hello` registers the frame by origin and platform, never its address. A frame that says hello from another document loses the report and runs of the document it left; what the new document began before its hello stays.
    - **Forgetting is per document.** A run belongs to the document it was begun in; a report belongs to its run's document, and one whose run is no longer known goes with any document of its frame. A page the frame shows again from the back/forward cache may run before it says hello (or without one), so forgetting the document it replaced never takes its run or report.
    - A document the registry has not seen also sets off a sweep of the tab's other frames, in the background, so the hello is answered at once. When a top frame loads another page, the old page's subframes vanish without a word.
    - The sweep sends each other frame `frame/ping` at its own `{frameId, documentId}`. It forgets a frame (its entry, and that document's report and runs) only when nothing receives there twice, `FRAME_PROBE_GRACE_MS` (1 s) apart.
    - Any answer keeps the frame, an error answer included, and so does a listener that gives none, or no answer within `FRAME_PROBE_TIMEOUT_MS` (2 s).
    - Arrival order never decides. A subframe of the new page may say hello, and report, before its top frame does; it answers, so it stays. A frame that says hello again from another document between the two asks is not forgotten in its place.
    - A frame/hello from a document already registered (a single-page app's route change) sets off nothing.
    - A panel's `panel/reports` and `panel/watch` set off the same check over all the tab's frames, one check at a time per tab. A page that holds none of our frames says no hello, for example a company page that embedded a form and was left for one without.
    - The panel's answer and its first push are what is known at once; a report the check removes reaches the panel port as one more `panel/update`.
    - **So a content script registers its onMessage listener before it sends `frame/hello`.**
  - `run/begin` mints a run per document and keeps its tab, frame, document, key and mode (`storage.session`, `runs:<runId>`). A frame's next run replaces its last.
    - It fetches `GET /profile` into memory and calls `POST /lookup {url: the frame's own address, includePostingText: true}`.
    - `policy.storedCopy` runs the policy patterns over the lookup's `postingText` (typed `StoredPostingText`): `hit` or `clear`, or `unavailable` when there is no copy or the lookup failed.
    - `policy.postingPage` reads the posting pages' `policy/hit` records for that key: `hit` (a hit is never cleared by a later clean reading), `clear`, or `unseen`.
  - `policy/hit` records a posting page's pattern id, or its clean reading, under the application's key.
    - A page can push addresses, each its own key, so at most `MAX_POLICY_RECORDS` (200) applications are kept: recording or reading one makes it the most recent, and the least recent beyond the cap goes. Their order is kept beside them, keys only (`policy-order`), checked against the records at each use, so a record it misses counts as the oldest.
    - At most `POLICY_HIT_LIMIT` (10 in 10 s) per frame. A reading sends one message per pattern it matched; past the limit the rest are answered `rate_limited` and dropped, which loses nothing once one hit is in (a hit is never cleared), and a lost clean reading leaves `unseen`.
  - `plan/request` hands the core's `planFrame` the run's canonical profile, the frame's fields (all of them, as context) and the keys to plan, the canaries, the settings and the vocabulary. Only a pass that plans a file field attaches the resume.
    - When the plan needs the sensitive part and the worker's memory does not hold it, the worker fetches `GET /profile/sensitive` at most once per run and plans again: a 403 makes the state `not-shared`, any other failure `unavailable`, and neither is retried in that run. A sensitive part already read for the same token and profile revision is reused, and is dropped with the profile after five idle minutes.
    - A dry run's answer never carries a write.
    - Every answer is checked against `PlanAnswerSchema` (its caps included) before it leaves.
    - **Repeatable sections (Phase 5):** `planFrame` gives each block of a section the request names its profile entry before anything is resolved (`core/steps/blocks.ts`: every entry's and every prefilled block's fingerprint, then `assignEntries`, so a block the site prefilled is filled from the entry it holds and nothing is duplicated), and the resolver fills the block from that entry (`ValueContext.blockEntries`), not from the one its place names; a block no entry takes gets none. A section whose request carries `entries` keeps them (each checked against the profile): the frame sends its assignment with a request over some of its fields only (a typeahead's results). Fields that wait for the LLM are planned again with the entries their blocks were given. Without `repeatables`, a block's fields use the entry its place names, as before.
  - `runstate/put` drops every canary that equals a profile value (the core's `persistableCanaries`), fetching the profile first when it is not in memory. When it cannot, it stores an empty `canarySet`.
    - Entries are checked on the way in and out, expire 24 h after `updatedAt`, and at most 20 are kept (the oldest go).
    - Its records are capped by `RUN_STATE_RECORD_CAPS` (`messaging/schemas.ts`), from the core's `MAX_STEPS` (30): `stepAttempts` and `filledFieldKeys` one entry per step; `repeatableCounters` three per step (experience, education, language); `aiTasks` 960 (`MAX_STEPS` × 8 × 4: a batch per pass, `MAX_PASSES` 8, for each of the four AI task kinds, per step; a step whose AI answers keep its loop going to `MAX_AI_PASSES`, snapshot round 2, uses more of that total). The count is checked before any entry is parsed.
    - **The whole is capped too**, because the record caps still let one RunState reach about 9 MB (30 steps × 500 keys × 600 characters), nearly all of `storage.local`'s 10 MiB quota, which settings share. `MAX_RUN_STATE_BYTES` (256 KiB, `background/runstate-store.ts`) bounds its JSON in UTF-8 bytes, as it would be stored, weighed before the canary check and before any write; past it the put is `too_large` and `runstate.too-large` logs the size in KiB.
      - **The figure:** the worst legitimate RunState, at the step guard (30 steps of 25 written fields at the longest key the core builds, about 140 characters; 100 reveal edges; 32 full-length canaries; every repeatable counter; four AI tasks a step), is about 160 KiB, and a real application (6 to 8 steps) stays under 60 KiB. 256 KiB leaves 60% over the first, and 20 RunStates at the cap take 5 MiB, half the quota.
    - The tab that stores an application's RunState last is the one that holds it (`tabrun:<tabId>`).

**Port `resume`** (content scripts only; same sender checks).
1. The content script posts `resume/request {runId, document?}` (Phase G: `document: 'cover-letter'` asks for the run's cover letter; absent, the resume).
2. The worker answers `resume/header {name, mime, size, sha256, chunkCount}` and `resume/chunk {index: 0, data}`.
3. After each `resume/ack {index}` it posts the next chunk; after the last ack, `resume/end`.
4. The content script answers `resume/done {ok}`, and the worker releases the bytes and disconnects.

- **Which resume:** the lookup's `suggestedResume`, otherwise the profile's `documents.resumeId` (named through `GET /resumes`). After a worker restart the run is still known, and the worker reads the lookup again from the port's own frame.
- **Refusals,** each a `resume/error {reason}` followed by a disconnect:
  - `resume-format-unsupported`: a legacy `.doc`, refused before any fetch, or a file TailorIT sends as `doc`;
  - `resume-unavailable`: no such run (or another frame's, or a dry run), no resume, a failed or timed-out fetch, a file over 10 MiB, a PDF that does not start with `%PDF-`, a DOCX without the ZIP signature or without `X-TailorIT-File-Kind: docx`, or bytes whose SHA-256 is not `X-TailorIT-Sha256`;
  - `resume-transfer-failed`: an acknowledgement out of order, an unexpected message, or the 60-second release.
- **From the content script:** `resume/error` (for example `pdf-only-input`, after a DOCX header for an input that takes PDF only) ends the transfer, and so does disconnecting.
- **Releasing:** the bytes go on `resume/done`, on an error or a disconnect, and at the latest 60 s after the fetch.
- **A cover letter** (Phase G) travels the same way: the run's ready letter only, a PDF only, its hash checked; see "Cover letters".

**Worker → content script** (`tabs.sendMessage` to `{frameId, documentId}`).

| Message | Sent when | The frame answers |
|---|---|---|
| `run/start {dryRun}` | `panel/fill`: to every registered frame of the tab that has a form; since jr6 (F3a), on a page with no form, to the tab's application frame alone (`wallFrameOf`), whatever it told, with `signIn: true` for Sign Up & Autofill (`panel.ts` `fill`) | `{}` |
| `run/continue`, `run/cancel` | `panel/continue`, `panel/cancel`: to the frames that reported. `run/continue` resumes a pause only and never answers a policy card; `run/cancel` stops a run, a policy card's wait included, or stops a paused one for good (`USER_STOPPED`, stored and reported: the Phase 5 review, C-S6). Since jr6 (F3a item 6) `run/cancel` also goes to the tab's application frame while it presses the site's Apply or waits for what the press brings (its `entering`), and the tab's entry grant ends (`panel.ts` `cancel`) | `{}` |
| `run/policyDecision {decision}` (`fill` or `cancel`) | `panel/policyDecision`: to that one frame, while its report is at `policy-notice` (since v4 no run waits there, and the frame answers `expired`) | `{}` once the decision settled its policy card (`fill` is Fill anyway, `cancel` is Cancel); `expired` when no run there waits at the card (`DoneAnswerSchema`) |
| `field/focus {fieldKey}` | `panel/focus` | `{}` |
| `field/describe {fieldKey}` | `panel/describe` | `{options: [{index, label}]}` (`FieldDescribeAnswerSchema`, at most 3): the visible labels of the row's suggested options, passed to the panel and never stored |
| `report/update {reports}` | Phase 6: at the frame's `panel/shown {open: true}` and on every change of the tab's reports, while the in-page panel is chosen (`settings.panel: 'in-page'`; since v5 Phase 1 `'page'`, to an embedded frame's in-page card, the sidebar reading its `panel` port): to the tab's panel frame alone, and only while its document is the registered one (the record goes with that document, the tab, or the frame's `panel/shown {open: false}`). Before Phase 6 every frame that reported received it, so one ATS frame saw the labels another frame of the tab reported; now only the frame the person opened the panel in does. A frame whose panel is closed keeps nothing of it | `{}` (not read) |
| `settings/changed {settings}` (`ContentSettingsSchema`: panel placement, auto-advance, the AI switches and the offer to save answers, as `frame/hello` gives them) | `settings/save` stored new settings: to every registered frame, of every tab, that has a form (the Phase 5 review, C-S2), and since the v5 Phase 1 review (F1) to every top frame of ours, whose sidebar follows "Where the panel shows" and "Default view". A frame that cannot be reached is skipped; its next run reads them at `run/begin` | `{}` (`DoneAnswerSchema`). The frame's run reads auto-advance from it from now on |
| `frame/ping` | another frame of the tab said hello from a new document: is this document still there? (`frame/hello` above) | `{pong: true}` (`PingAnswerSchema`); any answer counts |
| `draft/confirm {fieldKey}` | Phase 6, wave 2: `panel/confirmDraft`, to that one frame | `{}` once the run's waiting draft of that field is the person's (its row verified, reported again); `expired` when no such draft waits there, or the field no longer holds the draft as written or holds a canary (the Phase 6 review, E6: its row then says why) (`DoneAnswerSchema`) |
| `job/refresh {action}` (v2; `lookup` or `add`) | `panel/job` found no card for the tab's form frame (its lowest registered frame with a form), or `panel/addJob`: to that frame's document | `{}` once it relayed `job/lookup` or `job/add` and the worker answered that (`DoneAnswerSchema`); `expired` from a frame with no form of ours. A frame that does not relay, or is gone, makes the card `unavailable` |
| `job/postingText` (v2) | `panel/ask`, and (Phase G) `panel/letter`: to the tab's form frame | `{text, canaries, policy, names?}` (`JobPostingTextAnswerSchema`): the posting's visible text outside the form root, as a draft reads it ('' in a frame inside another page), the page's nearest 32 canaries, whether its visible text forbids AI, and the company and title its page title names. Passed to TailorIT with the question or the letter, never kept |
| `sidebar/open {reveal?}` (v5 Phase 1) | the toolbar icon's click under "Where the panel shows: On the page": to the tab's top frame, from the worker alone (`reveal`: the launcher shown again where the person hid it) | `{opening}` (`SidebarOpenAnswerSchema`): false where this frame shows no sidebar, or the place is Chrome's side panel now. A frame that answers nothing gets the content script first where `sites.ts` `injectTop` may run it, else Chrome's side panel |
| `applied/popup` (v5 Phase 1) | TailorIT recorded the tab's confirmed submission (POST /applied; never the agent's tab): to the tab's top frame, once per recorded submission | `{}` (`DoneAnswerSchema`) once "Application Submitted!" shows or waits for the tab to be visible; `bad_sender` from any sender but the worker's own script, `expired` outside the top frame or after the runtime stopped |
| `plan/ready {runId, reviews?}` (v5 Phase 2) | an AI answer or draft of the run came in on its task's stream, or a batch ended: to the run's own frame and document, once per burst (the run's id; since the review's security 5 `reviews`, at most 500 keys whose streamed answer or draft TailorIT then voided, never an answer) | `{}` (`DoneAnswerSchema`) once the run's wait for the AI polls at once (`AiWake`); `bad_sender` from any sender but the worker's own script, `expired` for a run that is not the frame's current one. Since the review, `reviews` for a run that already ended still flags its rows (`ai-reply-voided`) |
| `answer/read {fieldKey}` | Phase 6, wave 2: `panel/saveAnswer` or `answer/save`, to that one frame | `{optionSetHash, answer, company}` (`AnswerReadAnswerSchema`): the field's answer as it holds it now (its options' clean labels, never their places; a text; a lone box's state), its observed option-set hash, and the company the frame's own address names (`<platform>:<board>`, or null: the Phase 6 review, E5); or `{refused: 'unsupported' | 'canary' | 'sensitive'}` (`sensitive`: no plan named the field savable); `expired` when the field is not on the page. Passed to TailorIT, never kept or logged |
| `chain/leafFacts {facts}` (jr6, SHELL-B, ICIMS-17) | the tab's claim frame said its step facts (`chain/facts`): to each IForm leaf frame chained from it in the tab (the same family, its forms origin, `chain.ts` `leavesOf`), at that leaf's document; also when a leaf says hello after them | `{}` once that document holds them for its step machine (`content/chain-frame.ts` `holdChainFacts`: an IForm leaf its portal loaded, `loadedByPortal`; null clears them); `expired` where the frame holds none. From the worker alone (`runtime.ts` checks the sender); a document gone is no failure |

**Extension pages → worker** (`runtime.sendMessage`; the sender must be one of this extension's pages).

| Message | Answer (`data`) | Refusals |
|---|---|---|
| `ping` | `{pong: true}` | none |
| `panel/fill {tabId, dryRun}` | `{frames}` (`PanelSentAnswerSchema`): the frames that received `run/start` | `not_connected` |
| `panel/continue {tabId}`, `panel/cancel {tabId}` | `{frames}`: the toolbar's Continue (pauses only) and Cancel (since v4 the main Autofill button while a run waits, and the progress line's Cancel) | none |
| `panel/policyDecision {tabId, frameId, decision}` (`fill` or `cancel`; since v4 no panel sends it) | `{}` (`DoneAnswerSchema`), once that frame's policy card took the decision | `expired`: that frame is not registered, its report is not at `policy-notice`, or the frame answers that no run there waits at the card. `unavailable`: it did not answer, or its answer fails the schema |
| `panel/focus {tabId, frameId, fieldKey}` | `{}` | `expired`: that frame is not registered. `unavailable`: it did not answer |
| `panel/describe {tabId, frameId, fieldKey}` | `{options}` (`FieldDescribeAnswerSchema`) | `expired`, `unavailable` (no answer, or one that fails the schema) |
| `panel/reports {tabId}` | `{tabId, reports, page}` (`TabReportsAnswerSchema`): the tab's reports, one per frame, by frame id, as known when asked, and (v2) its page state `{support: 'form' \| 'no-form' \| 'none', platform, formKey}` from the frame registry (`formKey` changes with the form frame's document). It also starts the tab's frame check, whose removals reach the panel port | none |
| `panel/job {tabId, onLoad?}` (v2; `onLoad: true` since the v5 Phase 1 review, S1) | `{tabId, card}` (`PanelJobAnswerSchema`): the job card of the tab's form frame, from memory or read through that frame (`job/refresh`); `card` null without a form frame or a connection, `{state: 'unavailable'}` when TailorIT could not say. `onLoad` (set by the sidebar's transport for a sidebar that opened by itself, `asOpened`): a lookup as the page loads, `card` null in a private tab, before the disclosure as it reads now is acknowledged, with on-load lookups off, or once `LOOKUP_ON_LOAD_LIMIT` is spent ("v5 Phase 1", "The worker"). The card (`JobCardSchema`): the job's title, company, location, closed, posting time, pay and arrangement, the tracker status and archived, the resume a fill attaches (title, file kind, reason), its match (`ok` with counts and %, `no-keywords`, `unreadable`) and whether the address lets TailorIT add the job. Never an id, an address, the posting text or a file name | none |
| `panel/addJob {tabId}` (v2) | `{outcome}` (`AddJobAnswerSchema`): `added`, `saved`, `closed` (404), `unsupported` (400, or an address the extension knows TailorIT cannot add from, never sent), `not-allowed` (403: a connection made before `jobs:add`), `rate-limited`, `not-connected`, `unavailable`. The card is read again with `panel/job` | none |
| `panel/resumes {tabId}` (v2) | `{items: [{id, label, fileKind, kind, current}]}` (`ResumeListAnswerSchema`), from `GET /resumes`: `current` is the resume the tab's card attaches. Never a file name | `not_connected`, `unavailable` |
| `panel/setResume {tabId, resumeId}` (v2; null: TailorIT's own choice) | `{}`: the choice is kept for the tab in the worker's memory; the card's lookup, `run/begin` and `panel/addJob` pass it as `resumeId`, and a card read for another choice is not shown | none |
| `panel/ask {tabId, question, length}` (v2; `short`, `medium`, `long`) | `{outcome: 'answered', text, resumeLabel, policy}` or `{outcome}` (`PanelAskAnswerSchema`): `no-resume`, `sensitive`, `unusable`, `rate-limited`, `not-connected`, `unavailable`, `ai-off`. See "The custom question" below | `bad_message`: an empty question or one over 1,000 characters |
| `panel/letter {tabId, write}` (Phase G) | `{outcome}` (`PanelLetterAnswerSchema`): `yours`, `written`, `none`, `failed`, `ai-off`, `not-allowed`, `rate-limited`, `not-connected`, `unavailable`. See "Cover letters" | none |
| `panel/confirmDraft {tabId, frameId, fieldKey}` (Phase 6, wave 2) | `{}` (`DoneAnswerSchema`), once that frame confirmed the draft (`draft/confirm`) | `expired`: the frame is not registered, its report shows no draft of that field waiting for the person, or the frame says none waits. `unavailable`: no answer, or one that fails the schema |
| `panel/saveAnswer {tabId, frameId, fieldKey}` (Phase 6, wave 2) | `{outcome}` (`SaveAnswerAnswerSchema`): `saved`, or why not: `sensitive` (the extension's screen, or TailorIT's 400), `limit` (409), `unsupported`, `company-unknown`, `canary` (the frame's refusal), `not-allowed` (403). The answer is read from the frame (`answer/read`) and PUT at once, never kept | `not_connected`. `expired`: the setting is off, the frame is not registered, or its report does not offer Save my answer on that row (flagged, `detail.userEdited`, never a file nor a class whose saved answers are `never`). `rate_limited`, `unavailable` |
| `settings/get` | `{settings}` (`SettingsAnswerSchema`): the stored settings, or the defaults when none parse | none |
| `settings/save {settings}` | `{settings}`, as stored in `storage.local` (`settings:v1`), once `settings/changed` went to the frames with a form (since the v5 Phase 1 review, F1, and every top frame of ours) | `bad_message`. `bad_sender` from the sidebar (the review's S7) when the merged result lets the extension act more on its own (`raisesAutonomy`) |
| `auth/status` | `AuthStatusSchema`: `connection` (`connected`, `not-connected`, `expired`, `revoked`), `account` (name and email, read from `GET /session` into memory), `scopes`, `expiresAt`, `storageAccess` (`pending`, `restricted`, `refused`), `versionMismatch`, `pairingExpiresAt` | none |
| `auth/startPairing` | `{expiresAt}` (`StartPairingAnswerSchema`). The worker keeps a single-use state (32 random bytes, base64url) for 10 minutes and opens `<origin>/settings/autofill?tab=extension&connect=1&state=…`; the state is never in the answer | `unavailable`: the tab could not open |
| `auth/redeemCode {code}` | the new `AuthStatusSchema` | `bad_message`: not a code. `expired`: TailorIT answered 401 (wrong, used or expired). `rate_limited`, `unavailable` |
| `auth/disconnect` | the new `AuthStatusSchema`. The token is forgotten at once, then revoked on TailorIT (`DELETE /session`, best effort) | none |
| `runs/clear` | `{cleared}` (`RunsClearAnswerSchema`): every RunState and tab index removed | none |
| `telemetry/get` (Phase 6) | `{counts}` (`TelemetryAnswerSchema`): the anonymous counts in `storage.local`, one per combination of the five fields, each `{platform, fieldKind, matcherStage, outcome, tripwireTriggered, count}` and nothing else; none before the person opted in | none |
| `telemetry/clear` (Phase 6) | `{counts: []}` (`TelemetryAnswerSchema`), once every count is removed | none |
| `panel/notes` (v5 Phase 1) | `{closed}` (`PanelNotesAnswerSchema`): the header notes the person closed for good (`PANEL_NOTES`: `workday`), from `storage.local` `notes:v1` | none |
| `panel/closeNote {note}` (v5 Phase 1) | `{}` (`DoneAnswerSchema`), once the note's name is kept in `notes:v1` | `bad_message`: a note not in `PANEL_NOTES` |
| `panel/profileCheck` (the v5 Phase 1 review, S6) | `{gaps, history}` (`ProfileCheckAnswerSchema`): what the profile lacks, by name from closed lists (`gaps`: `first`, `last`, `email`, `phone`, `place`; `history`: `experience`, `education`), worked out by the worker from GET /profile alone (never the sensitive part); never a value | `not_connected`. `rate_limited`: its own key under `PROFILE_EDIT_LIMIT` (20 a minute). `unavailable` |
| `signup/status`, `signup/save {email, password?}`, `signup/delete`, `signup/import` (jr6, `messaging/page-schemas.ts`; `background/signup.ts`) | `SignupStatus {set, email, updatedAt, suggestedEmail, importable, notice?}`; `signup/delete` answers `{status, reverted}`, `signup/import` `{outcome: 'imported' \| 'none' \| 'weak', status}`. Never the password: none of them carries it back, and a save's password is in the request alone | `bad_message`: an email that is none, a password that misses a rule, or no password where none is saved. `unavailable`: the import with one saved here, without `credentials:read`, or TailorIT not reached. `bad_sender` from the sidebar's channel (none is in `SIDEBAR_PAGE_MESSAGES`) and from TailorIT's pages |

- **The policy card in the side panel** (until v4, which removed the card, D6; both messages stay, answered `expired`) (agreed with the content runtime): each frame whose run is at `policy-notice` gets a card of its own, and its Fill anyway and Cancel send `panel/policyDecision {tabId, frameId, decision: 'fill' | 'cancel'}` for that frame.
  - The worker sends it on as `run/policyDecision {decision}` to that one frame, and only while the frame's report is at `policy-notice`. The frame treats it exactly as its own card's buttons: `fill` is Fill anyway, `cancel` is Cancel.
  - So a frame stopped at `policy-notice` sends its report (status `policy-notice`, notice `policy`) before it waits.
  - The toolbar's Continue (`panel/continue`, then `run/continue` to every frame that reported) resumes pauses only: with one frame at the policy card and another at a CAPTCHA pause, it resumes the pause and the card keeps waiting for the person's own decision. The toolbar's Cancel still stops every reporting frame's run, a card's wait included, which writes nothing.
  - The page's own card takes trusted clicks only and decides locally; the panel's buttons are the panel's, on the extension's own page.

**Port `panel`** (extension pages only): how the side panel follows a tab.
- The page posts `panel/watch {tabId}`, and again with another id when the active tab changes. Only the latest watch counts.
- The worker posts `panel/update {tabId, reports, page}` (`PanelPortMessageSchema`) at once, then on every change of that tab's reports: a `report/put`, a frame loading another document, the frame check finding a document gone (each watch starts that check), the tab closing; and (v2) when a hello or a forgotten frame changes the page state that port was last sent. Every push for a tab goes through one queue per tab, its reports and page read inside it, so an older state is never posted last.
- (v2) The side panel reads its tab again when the tab finishes loading a page (`tabs.onUpdated`, status `complete`: no permission, no address), and the worker checks a tab's frames after Autofill, Continue and Cancel, so a strip never stays on a page our script is no longer in.
- (v2, the Phase E review) The worker posts `panel/changed {what: 'connection' | 'settings'}` on every open panel port when a token is stored or dropped (whichever window did it) and when the settings are saved. Nothing else is in it: the panel reads `auth/status` or `settings/get` again, and on a connection change drops the job card it holds at once (the card's read key counts these changes).

**The job card (v2)** (`background/job-cards.ts`, `content/job-relay.ts`, `ui/panel/{job.ts, JobCard.tsx}`).
- The worker has no page address; the form frame's content script has it as Chrome's `sender.url`. So `panel/job` sends the frame `job/refresh {action: 'lookup'}`, the frame relays it as `job/lookup` with no payload, and the worker looks the sender's address up and keeps the card. Both answers to the frame are `{}`: nothing TailorIT said reaches the page's process, a page cannot start a lookup (the worker takes a relay only while its own refresh for that document waits), and nothing is stored or logged but codes.
- The card is kept per document (tab, frame, document) for `JOB_CARD_TTL_MS` (10 minutes); it goes with its document, its tab, the token, or a change of the tab's chosen resume. Reads of one document are merged; a failed lookup is not kept. `run/begin`'s lookup becomes the card too (without its posting text), so a fill shows what it attaches with no request of its own.
- **Single-page applications** (the Phase E review): each address change in a document is counted by the content script, which says `frame/hello {…, nav}` again at each one while it shows a form. A new count forgets the document's card (and drops its refreshes in flight), and the page state's `formKey` carries the count, so the panel reads the card again for the posting shown now. A count only: no address is sent in a payload, stored or logged.
  - Chrome's `sender.url` for a content script is its document's address when the script started: history.pushState does not move it (the e2e spec 58 saw the previous posting looked up). So `senders.ts` takes the top frame's address from `sender.tab.url` (readable on the ATS hosts), which follows pushState, when it is on the document's own origin and an ATS address (`addressFrom: 'tab'`); every content message of the top frame, `run/begin`'s and the planner's included, then names the posting shown now. A subframe has no such address: once a single-page application moved it (its registry `nav` above 0), neither the card's relay nor `run/begin` looks it up (the card says `unavailable`; the fill goes on as with a lookup that failed).
- **Epochs** (the Phase E review): each refresh and `run/begin`'s lookup takes its document's epoch, which moves when the document or its tab is forgotten; a lookup answered after it moved is not kept, so a card never outlives its page. A relay that arrives after the token changed sends nothing (no lookup; Add answers `not-connected`), and an Add whose page moved on answers `unavailable`.
- **When TailorIT is asked:** as an application page whose form was found loads (v3 Phase 3, while connected: the hello's lookup above), while the side panel shows such a tab (the panel reads the card when the tab, the form frame's document, the connection or the tab's runs change), when the person adds or saves the job or changes the resume, and at Autofill or a dry run (since v4 no button: its message alone). Since v5 Phase 1 the sidebar reads it as the side panel does; one that opened by itself reads it as a page load would be looked up (`panel/job {onLoad: true}`, the Phase 1 review's S1: never in a private tab, before the disclosure as it reads now is acknowledged, or past `LOOKUP_ON_LOAD_LIMIT`). The first-run disclosure says so.
- **Token hygiene:** a new or dropped token drops every card and choice and every run's lookup, resume and chosen resume (`runs.dropTokenData()`); the token keeper's `generation` counts changes, and a lookup in flight across one is never kept (`run/begin`, the resume chooser, the card). The side panel's read key holds the connection's expiry, and it never shows a card read under another connection.
- **A malformed `match` drops only the match:** the server client reads the lookup with `match: unknown` and checks the match on its own (`server.lookup-match-dropped`), so the stored copy's policy check never hangs on it.
- **The resume the person chose** (Change): `panel/setResume` keeps it per tab; the card's lookup, `run/begin` and Add send it as `resumeId`, and the run attaches it first (`resume-choice.ts`: chosen, then the lookup's suggestion, then the profile's). The chooser reads the tab's choice at every ask, the resume port's included (one chooser for both, index.ts), so a Change made while a fill runs applies to every file it has not uploaded yet: the run's suggestion is asked again for the new choice. Change is offered also when the card has no resume to attach.

**The custom question (v2)** (`background/ask.ts`, `ui/panel/Ask.tsx`).
- `panel/ask` screens the question first (a sensitive topic or an attention check is `sensitive`, nothing sent); takes the tab's active resume (chosen, else the card's, else the profile's; none: `no-resume`); asks the form frame for the posting's visible text and the page's canaries (`job/postingText`, '' for an embedded form, never TailorIT's stored copy); and sends one draft question (`POST /ai/draft`, its cap 600, 1,500 or 3,000 characters) with the card's company and title. A task not finished within TailorIT's wait is polled for at most 120 s. A draft holding a canary or past its cap is `unusable`. Six questions a minute per tab.
- The question and the answer live in the side panel's memory only. It works in profile-only mode too (the person asked), with the posting's no-AI notice above the box: whenever a report of the tab says the posting forbids AI (`saysNoAi`: the policy card, or the notices `policy`, `policy-profile-only`, `ai-attestation`, `policy-stored-copy`), and, before any run, when the answer says so (`policy`: the form frame's visible text matched the policy patterns, `job/postingText`'s `policy`, or a report did).
- It follows the AI-drafts switch (`llm.drafts`): while it is off the box is disabled ("AI-written answers are turned off in Options") and the worker answers `ai-off`, sending nothing. A token change while it is asked sends nothing (`not-connected`).
- Any other message closes the port.

**External** (`runtime.onMessageExternal`): TailorIT's connect page only.
- `pair/complete {state, code}` is answered `{}` (`ok: true`) once the code is redeemed (`POST /connect/redeem`, no cookie, no Authorization header), the token is stored and `GET /session` is read.
- **Refusals:**
  - `bad_sender`: an origin that is not exactly TailorIT's, an address that is not `/settings/autofill`, or a state that is not the pending one (the pending one stays);
  - `expired`: no pairing pending, or it lapsed. The state is used once, whatever the redeem's outcome;
  - `bad_message`;
  - redeem's own: `expired`, `rate_limited`, `unavailable`.

**Where the worker keeps things** (value-free unless stated; the token only in IndexedDB):
- `storage.local`: `settings:v1`; `run:<applicationKey>`; `telemetry:v1` (Phase 6, only once the person opted in): `{counts}`, the anonymous counts; `notes:v1` (v5 Phase 1): `{closed}`, the names of the header notes the person closed. Since jr6 also `signup:v1` (Sign-up Information's record: the email, the password's AES-GCM ciphertext and nonce, the time saved) and `signup:notice` (one word: what the worker did by itself since the person's last save, import or delete).
- `storage.session`:
  - `frames:<tabId>:<frameId>` and `report:<tabId>:<frameId>`;
  - `tabrun:<tabId>`;
  - `queue:run` (v1.5 part 2, `queue-runner.ts` `QUEUE_RUN_KEY`): the Queue tab's run: its phase, timer and holds, and per job TailorIT's ids, its title and company, its application's key (a hash, never an address), its status, tab and times; since jr6 also each job's `entryAt` (Autofill given at a page with no form: F4a) and `quietAt` (a Filling tab with no fill report: SHELL-B), each a time or null; gone at a browser restart;
  - `chainfacts:<tabId>:<frameId>` (jr6, SHELL-B): an iCIMS portal claim frame's step facts for its document, `{documentId, applicationKey, facts}` (an id, a boolean and counts; no address), dropped with the tab;
  - `runs:<runId>`: tab, frame, document, key, mode, the outcome of the run's one sensitive fetch, and (Phase G) `letterWritten`, a flag that TailorIT's AI started writing its cover letter;
  - `policy:<applicationKey>`: pattern ids, and whether a clean reading was seen; at most 200;
  - `policy-order`: the application keys that hold a policy record, least recently used first;
  - `pairing`: the pending state and its expiry;
  - `panel-frame:<tabId>` (Phase 6): the frame that shows the tab's in-page panel, `{frameId, documentId}`;
  - `telemetry:counted` (the Phase 6 review, E8, only while the person opts in): the row keys each run counted, `{runs: [[runId, keys]]}`, the latest 20 runs, at most 2,000 keys each; keys only;
  - `matchcache` (Phase 6, wave 2): the accepted matches, `{entries}` least recently used first, at most 5,000: each a cache key and the chosen option's normalized label, stage, confidence, who accepted it and when, never an index;
  - `aitasks` (Phase 4): the AI tasks' ids by application and batch name (ids and hashes only; a pick batch's name hashes no profile text, its profile revision instead: the Phase 4 review, R7), each for TailorIT's hour, at most 720; folded into the application's `RunState.aiTasks` at every `runstate/put`, which `storage.local` keeps for RunState's 24 hours.
- IndexedDB `tailorit` › `secrets` › `auth`: the token, its id, expiry and scopes. The account's name and email stay in memory. Since jr6 also `signup-key`: the job-site password's AES-GCM key, made with extractable false.
- Memory only: the profile and its sensitive part (dropped after 5 idle minutes, or when the token changes), the saved answers read with the profile (dropped with it), each run's lookup, its drafts and the company its frame's address names, the accepted-match candidates per application, the resume's bytes, and (v2) the job cards per document and the resume chosen per tab; (Phase G) each run's cover letter (its id and state) and its bytes for the transfer, and the job card's cover-letter answers per document. Since jr6: each tab's entry grant (`sign-up.ts`: the documents it reached, the press's keys, its times and step count) and, for one answer, a decrypted job-site password: its decrypted bytes are zeroed once read (`signup-vault.ts` `plain.fill(0)`), and the password string, which JavaScript cannot clear, is held only for the `signin/credential` answer that carries it to the frame, until garbage collection takes it. The Queue tab's run is in `storage.session` (`queue:run`, above).

## Phases (the owner's process: after each, a summary, test results, known gaps and exactly what I need; then STOP)
| Phase | Extension (`TailorIT-autofill`) | TailorIT (`autofill` worktree) | Needs from the owner first |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 Plan + scaffold | The repo and tooling; docs (`docs/SPEC.md` verbatim, the design reports as corrected); the type definitions; entrypoints that build for Chrome and Edge; one smoke test per harness; check-build; the CI file | The v1.1 plan restored to `docs/plans/` (docs only) | Approval of this plan, and your word on the interpretations above |
| 1 Matcher | Normalization; the vocabularies, built by a script from pinned public sources and committed as JSON; the entity canonicalizers that work from text and the vocabulary (institution, degree, field, country, region); the cascade with the distinctive-token guard; degree granularity; typeahead query variants; the resolver's rule tables; golden tests (62 positive, 28 negative, the injected-option cases included), whose cases build canonical profile fragments and intended values directly | none | Approval to download the vocabulary sources once: IPEDS, ROR, CIP 2020 and CLDR (public data, pinned by SHA-256; the build itself never touches the network) |
| 2 Guard, scanner, filler | Guard (pure and DOM); trap fixtures 1-11 (trap 8 in browser mode, with a fake AI client); scanner and filler for text, select, radio, checkbox and file; the generic adapter, plus the Lever and Greenhouse adapters and URL parsers, from snapshots; pairing and the server client; `canonicalizeProfile` over the contract; a plain report list in the side panel; the snapshot converter and the capture hotkey | The contract; pairing and the token middleware (`0801`); the profile and its seed (`0802`); the lookup (Greenhouse, Lever); the resume file; Settings › Autofill (Profile, Extension) | Lever and Greenhouse snapshots (see the Greenhouse question above) |
| 3 Reactive loop | settle(), the reactive loop, the reveal graph, the oscillation guard, synthetic fixtures | none | The `ai_tasks` migration choice (Risk 11), so Phase 4 can start |
| 4 Widgets | Combobox, multi-select, remote typeahead; Greenhouse (new widgets) and Ashby adapters; the LLM tiebreak and classification wired | The `ai_tasks` rebuild (`0004`, all three kinds), pick and classify, fake-CLI scenarios, the Ashby URL parser | Greenhouse and Ashby snapshots with dropdowns open |
| 5 Multi-step | The state machine with RunState persistence, repeatable sections, resume-parse reconcile, sign-in, CAPTCHA and email-code pauses; the Workday adapter and URL parser | The Workday URL parser | Workday snapshots of every step and the sign-in page, taken with a throwaway candidate account |
| 6 Review | The FillResult UI in the side panel and the in-page panel (a setting picks one), highlights, policy and tripwire notices, the drafts flow (trap 8 end to end, trap 1's draft clause), saved answers, local anonymous counters | Saved answers (`0803`, Settings tab), drafts | none |

## Folder structure (`F:\Develop\TailorIT-autofill`)
```
package.json  package-lock.json  .npmrc  wxt.config.ts  eslint.config.mjs  vitest.config.ts  playwright.config.ts
tsconfig.base.json  tsconfig.json  tsconfig.core.json  tsconfig.core-tests.json  tsconfig.tools.json
THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md (CLDR, ROR, NCES notes)   .github/workflows/ci.yml
docs/      SPEC.md ARCHITECTURE.md CONTRACT.md FIXTURES.md PRIVACY.md   design/{core,runtime,tailorit}.md
scripts/   vocab-build.ts  contract-pull.mjs  mhtml-to-fixture.mjs  verify.mjs  e2e.mjs  check-build.mjs  proc.mjs
vocab/     sources.json (url, release, sha256, licence)  curated/*.json (aliases, flagships, system patterns, referral, EEO)
           .sources/ (downloads, git-ignored)  reports/collisions.txt (committed, for review)
src/
  contract/tailorit/  TailorIT's contract copied byte for byte + v1/ (contract.json, fixtures) + SOURCE.json
  core/            PURE: no DOM, no chrome.*, no clock, no randomness. enums.ts, confidence.ts (every threshold), purity.test.ts
    types/         field, intent, match, fill, profile, settings, saved, run, telemetry (the Phase 0 definitions)
    text/          fold, normalize, abbreviations, qualifiers, tokens, similarity (Jaro-Winkler, token set), sha256, keys, untrusted
    vocab/         data/*.json (generated, committed), createVocab, lookup
    profile/       canonicalize (Phase 2), entity canonicalizers (Phase 1), names, degree parsing, dates
    matcher/       cascade, exact, alias, fuzzy, guardTokens, degree, mention, polarity, range, tiebreak, other, cache, typeahead, golden, candidates and searchLimits (v4)
    guard/         patterns, tripwire, canary, attention, policy, injected, provenance, markAll, honeypot, write, ready
    resolver/      categories, autocomplete, nameId, labelRules, saved, classify (with SERVER_CATEGORY_MAP), values, policy, drafts, resolve
    multiselect/   hints, plan, verify
    loop/          bookkeeping (Requirement D)
    steps/         signature, machine (Requirement C), repeatable, prefill
    telemetry/     events
  dom/             thresholds (every DOM timing and size), visibility, camouflage, visible-text (with the option and placeholder
                   readers), raw-scan, labels, kinds, proxy, constraints, sections, values, pause-detect, scan, settle,
                   composed, entrance, account-section, sign-in-form,
                   time (the content side's only timers), fillers/{text,select,choice,file,date,verify,not-submit},
                   bridge-protocol and bridge (the page-world bridge's protocol and the content script's client), widgets/
  page/            bridge (the page-world bridge, run in the page's own world; it imports only dom/bridge-protocol)
  adapters/        types (Phase 0); generic/ and lever/, greenhouse/ (Phase 2), ashby/ (Phase 4), workday/ (Phase 5), workable/ (v3 7a),
                   icims/ taleo/ successfactors/ oracle/ (v3 6/7b), site/ (v3 8), smartrecruiters/, configured/ (the new ATSs' layer)
                   and the fifteen new ATSs' folders (snapshot build); url.ts in each is pure; address, bridge-address,
                   ats-address, tailorit-address and chain (pure address readings)
  content/         runtime, orchestrator, pass, steps, transfer, report, ui/{shadow,launcher,toasts (notices until v4),ai-edits,panel,styles}
                   (v5 Phase 1: ui/{edge (the pill),sidebar,push,landed,applied}, and card.tsx, the in-page card's views,
                   which only the sidebar's bundle imports)
  background/      router, senders, server-client, auth, planner (runs the core), profile-cache, resume-transfer, ai-tasks, cover-letters (Phase G)
                   (translates the core's requests to TailorIT's DTOs and back), runstate-store, frames, reports, settings,
                   telemetry, timers (the worker's only timers)
  messaging/       envelope, schemas (the whole catalogue, zod, strict, capped), channels, ports
  platform/        types, chrome.ts (the only file that touches browser.*), fake.ts, memory.ts, sentinel.ts (v3 Phase 8),
                   sidebar.ts (v5 Phase 1: the sidebar's PagePlatform over the top frame's content channel)
  shared/          zod-setup (jitless; every entrypoint's first import), hosts (the one ATS list), log (codes and numbers only),
                   settings, report, copy (every sentence a person sees: PAGE_COPY the content script's, COPY every page's;
                   since v5 Phase 1 the reasons, statuses, provenances and stages are COPY's alone), sidebar (v5 Phase 1)
  ui/              report/ (presentational React: props in, callbacks out; one TS-string stylesheet), panel/, options/,
                   page/, brand/ (with Inter since v5 Phase 1), sidebar/ (v5 Phase 1: the sidebar's mount)
  entrypoints/     background.ts  content-script.ts (unlisted, listed by wxt.config.ts)  page-bridge.content.ts (world MAIN)  sidepanel/  options/  capture/ (dev builds only)
                   sentinel.ts (v3 Phase 8) and sidebar-app.ts (v5 Phase 1), unlisted scripts the worker runs
fixtures/          smoke/  traps/01..11  synthetic/{conditional-reveal,async-options,remote-typeahead,wizard-4-step,react-controlled,widgets}
                   lever/  greenhouse/  ashby/  workday/  (<case>.html + <case>.expect.json)   _incoming/ (raw .mhtml, git-ignored)
e2e/               fixtures.ts  routes.ts  helpers/ (spies, reports, pairing, stop-worker)  mock-server/server.mjs (TailorIT mock, port 3190)  specs/
test/              repo-level tests: the SPEC.md hash, every entrypoint's first import, the contract goldens, one project per test file
```
- **Where tests live:**
  - unit tests sit beside the code (`*.test.ts`); browser-mode DOM tests are `*.browser.test.{ts,tsx}`;
  - the Vitest projects: `core` (`src/core/**` and `src/adapters/**/url.test.ts`), `dom` (every `*.browser.test.*`) and `ext` (every other test under `src/` plus `test/`);
  - a repo test checks that each test file belongs to exactly one project.
- **zod** is imported at run time only by `src/contract/**`, `src/messaging/**` and `src/shared/**`.
- **Import rules** (tsconfig and ESLint):
  - `core` imports nothing outside itself, apart from `import type` from `src/contract/tailorit`.
  - `dom` imports `core` (its pure helpers, such as the key builders, `cutInjected` and `normalize`, as well as its types) and nothing else; `adapters` import `dom` and `core`.
  - `content` wires `dom`, `adapters`, `core` and the content platform. `content/ui/panel.tsx` alone may import `src/ui/report` and React; nothing else in `content` imports React. (Since v5 Phase 1 that file is `content/ui/card.tsx`, which only `entrypoints/sidebar-app.ts` imports, so the content script holds no React: `test/content-no-react.test.ts`.)
  - `background` imports `core`, `messaging` and the worker platform, never `dom`. `ui` uses messaging only.
  - Only `platform/chrome.ts` touches `browser.*`.
  - `page` (the page-world bridge) imports only `dom/bridge-protocol`, which imports nothing.
  - Timers exist only in `dom/time.ts` and `background/timers.ts`; fetch timeouts use `AbortSignal.timeout`.

## Types (Phase 0's definitions, in `src/core/enums.ts` and `src/core/types/*.ts`)
Closed lists are `as const` arrays (TailorIT's convention; no TS enums). The full lists (field categories, rule ids, degree types) are in `docs/design/core.md`.
```ts
// enums.ts
export const PLATFORMS = ['greenhouse', 'lever', 'ashby', 'workday', 'workable', 'icims', 'taleo', 'successfactors',
  'oracle', 'jobvite', 'jazzhr', 'bamboohr', 'rippling', 'breezy', 'careerplug', 'pinpoint', 'paycor', 'hibob', 'ultipro',
  'dayforce', 'paylocity', 'dice', 'clearcompany', 'adp', 'site', 'smartrecruiters'] as const;
                    // v3 Phases 7a, 7b and 8 ('site', a company's own page); the snapshot build of 2026-11-06
export const FIELD_KINDS = ['text', 'textarea', 'select', 'radio', 'checkbox', 'checkbox-group', 'combobox',
```
