An option a fresh read no longer shows but the widget shows chosen (a chip) stays in the field's reading, at its place, so its chip is still recognised (R5);
      - a group's member is clicked, and its `aria-checked` or `aria-pressed` read back. A typeless button inside a form is refused by `assertNotSubmit`, so the field is flagged `needs-your-click`.
    - **Remote typeahead** (`typeahead.ts`, and `searchField` in `pass.ts`):
      - At most four queries (since v4 `MAX_SEARCH_QUERIES`, six, and a 15 s budget: "v4" above; since snapshot round 2 four again, three styles and the list's "Other": "Snapshot round 2" above), in `typeaheadQueries`' order, one at a time. Each is its own guarded write: the DOM guard, then `guardWriteInFrame` on that one query.
      - A query is typed with the setter and one `input` event, never a key. No `change`, `focusout` or `blur` is sent while it searches.
      - The results are awaited (the list's own busy signs cleared, then a quiet window, capped at `TYPEAHEAD_QUERY_CAP_MS`) and sent to the worker as the field with its results as its options. `planFrame` chooses one only in the fill band, so an injected label's clean part (0.84 at most, always flagged) never is. The choice passes `guardWriteInFrame` before its click.
      - If nothing is chosen, the box gets back what it held, and the field is flagged with the last reason (`no-match`). A page that does not react at all to the first query (no mutation until the cap, and its list shows what it showed before: react-select filters a local list inside our own `input` event, before the wait watches it) is flagged `needs-your-click`, and the search stops there.
      - The box holds our own query, which is never a choice: after the click on a result, the page must answer it itself (change the box's value, mark the result `aria-selected`, name it as `aria-activedescendant`, or close its list). Otherwise it ignored the click: the box gets back what it held, and the field is `needs-your-click` (the Phase 4 review, R3).
      - React first: a widget its adapter declares for the page-world bridge has the matched result, listed now, chosen by its own React code before the click (its clean label as the frame read it), once per write. What the widget shows counts only once the page answered the choice itself (the box no longer holds our query, or its list closed: R3), read back with one more bounded wait. Shown: verified. The page answered, or the widget shows another answer, but not the result: chosen, not verified, and no click follows. Nothing changed: the click follows as above, and after a choice the bridge said it made it never ends as "nothing chosen". A search is never chosen through the bridge before its results exist.
    - **A long list searched for its planned option** (2026-11-01; `searchesList` and `searchForOption` in `dom/widgets`, `searchOption` in `pass.ts`, the pure `core/matcher/listSearch.ts`): a combobox its adapter declared whose text box filters its list as typed (`aria-autocomplete` list or both), with `WIDGET_SEARCH_MIN_OPTIONS` (50) options or more, or a list read only in part, has an option the planner chose from the profile found by typing rather than by scrolling to it: Greenhouse's phone country (the live form's 244 countries) and its country questions (about 200).
      - The planner still plans the option on the list's whole read (the waiting path's), as for any combobox; nothing is matched again.
      - The query is the option's own words as the page shows them, its injected text cut, without the dial code a phone country picker shows beside a country's name (`optionQuery`: "Canada" for "Canada +1"; `core/matcher/dialCode.ts`). It is one guarded write, exactly as a remote typeahead's query is (`queryGuard`, shared with `searchField`: a pause or Cancel refuses it, then the DOM guard on a fresh scan, then `guardWriteInFrame` on the query, the write's origin with transform `typeahead.query`), typed after the widget's one declared activation.
      - Of what the list then shows, the one result whose label reads as the planned option's label as the page shows it, injected text and all, and enabled, is clicked through `assertNotSubmit` and read back (`searchedOption`: none, or two, choose nothing). The click must be answered by the page itself, as a typeahead's.
      - Not searched: a short list, an option the widget shows already (`already-correct`), one no longer at its place (`options-changed`), a label under three characters, and any origin but the profile's: an llm-pick is checked again on the whole list right before its click (R1). A search that finds no such result, that the page does not answer, or whose list changed gives the box back what it held, closes the list, and the option is chosen from the whole list as before; a Guard's refusal ends it, blocked or flagged.
    - **Events:** the fillers' list only. A trusted `scroll` counts too, except the browser's own scroll on an element whose `scrollTop` the code set as a property: the DOM testkit's `logEvents` records every such set and marks that scroll `scrollTopSet`, and the e2e spies, which cannot see a set made in the extension's world, mark a scroll on a listbox, the one element the helpers scroll (the Phase 4 review, R8).
    - **Greenhouse's react-selects** (2026-11-01, from the capture of six live forms; docs/FIXTURES.md "The Greenhouse widget capture"): every "Select..." is react-select v5 inside a Greenhouse wrapper that holds whether its menu is open. Its `onMouseUp` toggles the menu, a choice and react-select's `onBlur` close it, and react-select's own opening on a mousedown, a focus or typing is ignored, so a `click()` opened nothing and every select ended flagged (or, filtering as typed, was taken for a search).
      - The adapter declares each `input.select__input[role=combobox]` inside a `.select__container`'s `.select-shell` (`widgetFor`): the input is the opener; the activation one bubbling `mouseup` (`WidgetDecl.activation`); its `aria-expanded` says whether the list is open (`WidgetDecl.expanded`), and the `mouseup`, which toggles, is sent only while it says "false"; its `.select__control` is the box a person sees (`WidgetDecl.box`: react-select draws the input transparent once it shows an answer, and sizes an empty one to a space of the page's font, under 4 px wide in a narrow web font such as Roboto; the field stays visible by that box only while the input is transparent by its own opacity alone, or narrow by its own width alone and drawn inside the box. Until that second rule, 2026-11-01, every select of the live BeyondTrust form, `fixtures/greenhouse/beyondtrust-job`, was taken for a hidden control and none was filled); `.select__menu-notice--loading` and `.select__loading-indicator` are its busy signs (`WidgetDecl.busy`), so "Loading..." is never read as options and the empty-list wait never gives up while School's first page loads; its `.select__single-value` is its answer (`WidgetDecl.shownAnswer`), the phone country's by its flag (`.iti__flag.iti__<code>` named in English with its dial code: "Canada +1", never "United States +1"; a name the list spells otherwise, the capture's "Turkey +90" that the browser names "Türkiye", is the one option of that dial code when no other shows it, else a label no option has).
      - The phone's country (244 countries, "United States +1" first; since snapshot round 2 labelled "Country code" and read as the phone's own country, `contact.phoneCountry`, never the address's, chosen from its stored list before the number: "Snapshot round 2", Greenhouse's phone) and a country question are long lists their own text box filters, so the profile's country, when the bridge does not choose it, is typed by its name and its one result clicked (above: "A long list searched for its planned option"). intl-tel-input also picks the phone's country by itself from a number typed with its dial code (the live BeyondTrust form: "+1 512 555 0147" made it "United States +1", "+44 20 7946 0958" "United Kingdom +44"). Read by its flag, that pick is `already-correct` only when it is the profile's country: a Canadian's "+1 512 555 0188", which intl-tel-input reads as the United States', ends with Canada typed and chosen (flagged `prefill-conflict`, as any answer the page held). A country chosen from the list makes Greenhouse draw a +1 number nationally ("(512) 555-0188"), its "+1" then shown by the country alone: the planner reads that as the planned number (below), so the phone is written once, as the profile writes it, and never typed again with its code.
      - Kinds: Location (`#candidate-location`) and School (`#school--N`) are `typeahead-remote`; a `select__value-container--is-multi` is a `multiselect`; every other one a `combobox`, which a remote reading never turns into a search (a declared kind stands, so a Yes/No, an EEO or a choice category takes it). A declared search is read as one without being opened. react-select's required shim and intl-tel-input's hidden country search are never declared, and never visible.
      - React first (docs/SPEC-AMENDMENTS.md §12): every react-select is declared for the page-world bridge (`WidgetDecl.bridge: 'react-select'`), so a planned option is chosen by react-select's own `selectOption` (the capture's props log: the Select sits 16 fibers above its input, with `selectOption`, its options and a `getOptionLabel`), the long lists without typing, a multi-select's picks one command each, a search's listed result once matched; no option is clicked and no list opened but to be read. The rest of this paragraph is the fallback: choosing is `click()` on the option, closing `focusout` and `blur`; a multi-select's menu closes after each pick, so each pick opens it again. A remote search is opened with its one `mouseup` before its first query (a closed react-select shows no results, and School sends nothing), and its first answer awaited, so the results compared are each query's own. Location's results are a geocoder's places ("Austin, Texas, United States"), which the core reads by their parts against the person's place (`matcher/place.ts`, docs/design/core.md §4.11): the city theirs, their region by its code or name, nothing conflicting; "Austin, TX" chooses Austin in Texas, never another state's. Its list shows only the results whose label holds the typed text (react-select's own filter; the live BeyondTrust form, 2026-11-01), so the geocoder's answer to "Austin, TX" is never listed: a place is searched as the profile writes it, then with its region's name ("Austin, Texas"), then by its city alone (`typeaheadQueries`).
      - Readiness: the page's React attaches 1.8 to 3.1 s after the form shows, and nothing answers before. The run's first declared activation that the page does not answer at all waits once for it (`WIDGET_READY_CAP_MS`, 4 s, or until the form root has changed and been quiet `WIDGET_READY_QUIET_MS`, or has left the page: a rebuilt form) and is sent once more; a widget the page replaced meanwhile is `gone` and found again on the next scan. One record for the run (`WidgetReadiness`, the loop's), so no later widget waits.
      - A form drawn again whole (Twitch's page is drawn again once its first render fails, the form element, the adapter's form root, included): the run never holds the detached root. Its form root is read at each use (`PassEnv.root`, `LoopEnv.root` and every scan of the orchestrator go through `rootNow`, the adapter's `detect` again), and the loop's rescans scan the root found then, so the loop's next scan reads the new form: the fields the rebuild emptied are resets, written again, and the selects the new React answers are read and chosen. The runtime takes the new root too (`RunEnv.rootMoved`, and `pageNow` before a run, a Show on page or the posting text) and anchors the launcher to it; a form drawn again while no run is on reaches it through the UI's layout (the old root's removal: `PageUiHandlers.rootGone`), so the launcher never stays hidden on a detached root, and an embedded form's frame check still sees it.
      - Checkboxes stay native: `click()` on the box, never `.checked`.
      - Never pressed (`neverPress`): see the Filler's "Never submits".
    - **Ashby** (v3 Phase 2, 2026-11-01, from the live capture of nine forms; docs/FIXTURES.md "The Ashby capture"; `src/adapters/ashby/`): a client-rendered React form with no `<form>`, every question an entry `[data-field-path]` whose title (`.ashby-application-form-question-title`, a `<label>` whose `for=` reaches only the text, email, tel, url, number, textarea and file inputs) names it, and every change saved to Ashby's server about 0.5 s later (docs/PRIVACY.md).
      - **Root and readiness:** `div#form[role=tabpanel]` once it holds `.ashby-application-form-container` and an entry. The form is drawn 4.8 to 6.8 s after the page loads, and again after the overview's Application tab changes the address: the adapter says so (`quirks.formLateMs`, 30 s, the runtime's cap), and the content runtime watches the document for it until then (`content/late-form.ts`: one look after each burst of mutations), then mounts the launcher and tells the worker. Meanwhile no other adapter may claim the page (`lateAdapters`): the generic adapter, which takes any page with a visible control, only once the wait is over without a form. The embed (`<Org>/<id>?embed=js`, then `/application?embed=js`) is the same app in an iframe; its three spellings share one application key (`adapters/ashby/url.ts`).
      - **Questions** (the scanner's adapter hooks, below): `labelFor` the entry's title for any control and group (an Education block's controls their own sub-labels; the SMS consent's radios its consent text), `labelSuffix` "month" and "year" for an Education block's lists, `descriptionFor` the question's description (and a one-box question's title, its box being named by its one option), `requiredFor` the title's hashed `_required_` class (drawn `::after "*"`), `formatFor` MM/DD/YYYY for the date box (ISO is saved a day early), `checkedOf` the span's `data-checked` beside an opacity-0 radio or checkbox. Sections: each section container by its `h2`, else its header description's first line (the survey's "U.S. EQUAL EMPLOYMENT OPPORTUNITY INFORMATION …"), else as the section before it; the root by the Application tab. The uploader "Autofill from resume" is `excluded` (it rewrites the form on Ashby's server).
      - **Yes/No:** `.ashby-application-form-input-yesno` is a `widgetRoots` element, declared a `'radio'` widget whose `members` are its two `button[data-option]`; `click()` answers it, `aria-pressed` reads it back, a pressed member is never clicked (that clears it), and its `display:none` checkbox is the widget's declared part (`WidgetDecl.parts`), no field but counted with the controls left out.
      - **Radio and checkbox groups:** fieldsets with no legend, labelled by the adapter, so a fieldset of checkboxes the adapter labels is one group whatever its boxes are named (each by its option's label, which repeats across questions on a page with no form); each input at opacity 0 behind its `label[for]` (the label proxy), `click()`ed, never `.checked`, read back by `checkedOf`.
      - **Comboboxes** (`input.ashby-application-form-input-autocomplete[role=combobox]`): Location (`_systemfield_location`, or a field the page's React calls a `Location`: the bridge's `react.field`, `{type, path}` from `props.field`; live, the bridge answered only for a ValueSelect (multiverse's, `{"type":"ValueSelect"}`), so the custom-Location path is read from the code, unconfirmed; an unanswered question is asked again, never kept as "not a Location") and an Education block's School are `typeahead-remote`; every other one, a ValueSelect of 9 options or more, a `combobox`. The opener is the toggle button (`click()`; a click on the input opens nothing), `aria-expanded` on the input says whether it is open, the list sits in a floating-ui portal named by the input's `aria-controls`, an option is chosen by `click()`, and the answer is the input's value, never the highlighted first result's `aria-selected` (`highlightIsNoChoice`: a typeahead counts that result's `aria-selected` as the page's answer only when the click set it; a widget that declares nothing keeps the older rule, where any mark is an answer). No box is declared: the input is judged as any control. `focusout` and `blur` close nothing, so `keys: ['Escape']` closes a list, only while it says it is open. While a list is open floating-ui marks the rest of the page `aria-hidden`, which the scanner reads as hidden, so a search closes its list before each later query's guard (`hidesFormWhileOpen`). A School result shows its name, country and domain side by side, and its box then holds the name alone: each result is labelled by its name (`optionLabel`). "No results" while a search loads is a list of no options, waited through as still loading, and a search still showing it after 1.5 s is read as empty (`emptyAfterMs`), not waited on to the query's 3 s cap.
      - **Education:** the repeatable education section (`repeatables`: its blocks and "+ Add Education"); its month and year lists are native selects (value setter, `input`, `change`), each month before its year (choosing a month sets this year), their dates answered by the core's `datePartChoice` (for a list whose label ends "month" or "year": the year by its digits; the month by the option the read list shows for it, by its name, abbreviation or number, "Aug", "08", "08 - August", else flagged when the list names other months, else its English name; Greenhouse's "Start date month" react-selects the same, and its "Start date year" text boxes take the four digits). The start's years end at the end year once one is chosen, so the start year's list changes after the end's write: the read-back finds the planned option at its place in the list as it is then (`content/verify.ts`, LC-9).
      - **Uploads:** only a file input inside a question's entry is an upload question (`uploadWidgets`); any file input outside every entry, the uploader's whatever its classes, is `excluded`. Ashby uploads a file the moment it goes in, so the run waits for Ashby's word (`uploadState`, `dom/fillers/upload.ts`, event-driven, at most 10 s): the read-only probe of 2026-11-02 (plaid's form, every upload aborted) showed, within 30 ms, the question's file item (its name, its Delete file button) and the toast "Error<name> failed to upload" together, so the item alone proves nothing. Failed when a toast names the file; confirmed when the item names it with its Delete button, nothing in the question is busy, no failure toast shows and the page has been quiet 1 s; pending at the cap. A failed or unconfirmed file is flagged (`upload-failed`, `upload-not-confirmed`), never verified, and read again at verification. A successful upload was never captured live (no live upload is allowed), so a slow failure that comes after that quiet second is caught only at verification, if its toast is still shown.
      - **The posting:** the overview's description (`postingText`: `#overview[role=tabpanel] .ashby-job-posting-description`, drawn about 6.6 s after the page loads) is the posting; the posting page is read once it is drawn (the runtime's late watch, as for the form). The Application tab shows none (its left pane of facts, some 2,000 characters, and its header are no posting), so the form frame reads only that element as the posting beside its form, and the couldn't-read notice shows there when nothing else was read.
      - **Saves as it is filled** (`autosaves`, docs/PRIVACY.md): while the person's setting keeps AI drafts for their confirmation, a draft is not written until their Confirm draft (`llm-draft-held`; `content/orchestrator.ts`).
      - **Never pressed:** the uploader and everything in it, "Upload File", "Delete file", an Education block's Delete, the Overview and Application tabs, "Apply for this Job"; Submit is identified (`submitButton`). The SMS consent follows Consent boxes (v3 Phase 3): under All agreements, the default, its Yes is chosen, and that one click sends `ApiSubmitCandidateTextingConsent` at once (docs/PRIVACY.md); under Required only it is never clicked. Its wording ("Check Yes or No to indicate your agreement to receive text message updates") is a radio that asks for consent (`labelRules.ts` `CONSENT_ASK`), so it reads as `consent.sms`, its Yes chosen by polarity. After Submit the same address shows `.ashby-application-form-success-container` (`ashbySubmitted`, for v3 Phase 4).
      - **jr8 (the owner's snapshot10, Honeydew's form, 2026-11-13):** a Yes/No the page shows answered (a button `aria-pressed="true"`; the hidden checkbox beside it, never read, not checked) whose question the profile does not answer is the person's (a list with a choice of its own: `answers.ts` `choiceOptions`), so its row stayed an empty circle until their next input; the step's rows now read it filled at FILLED (`orchestrator.ts` `pressedIn`: an open row of a toggle-button group showing a pressed member gets `detail.filledByYou`; never a native radio's or a select's default, a trap, a hard stop or an attention check, and auto-Next's counts read the rows without it). "What is your primary state of residence?" (a ValueSelect of the states' two-letter codes in a `fieldset._fieldEntry_` inside an unclassed `[data-field-path]` entry, a `combobox` like any other) is the state of residence by the rules (`label.residence-state`: the AI's screen reads the codes as naming the TN visa); the region picks its code from a list read whole, and a long list searched types the region's name, then its code (`matcher/candidates.ts`), as "Texas" filters a list of codes to nothing. Tests: `src/content/jr8-ashby.browser.test.ts`, `src/core/plan/jr8-region.test.ts`.
  - **As built (Phase 4, the LLM steps):** the core asks, the worker submits and translates, the content script waits and writes. Contract: TailorIT `autofill` `a1a3e9b` (`src/contract/tailorit/ai.ts`).
    - **The core** (`planFrame`): a single answer the cascade hands to the tiebreak (settings.llm.tiebreak) and a field nothing classified, not even a saved answer (settings.llm.classify), are `awaiting`, each with its question in `ask` and the row that stands until the answer. A tie whose pick could never be written is not asked: the field's own confidence times a pick's 0.75 must reach the flag band (0.6), which a field only the LLM classified (0.70) never does (the Phase 4 review, R6); nor is a list read only in part (R9). With the run's answers (`PlanInput.ai`), a verdict completes the match (`completeTiebreak`: only the item it was given for, never a hard-conflict or injected candidate, the label hash unchanged), and a valid pick is written as `llm-pick {taskId, pickedCandidate, labelHash}` at 0.75 at most, always flagged (`llm-pick-needs-review`); the frame's `guardWriteInFrame` checks `llmPickStillValid` again before the write. A set's values and a typeahead's results never go to the LLM.
    - **Classification** (`src/core/resolver/classify.ts`): `screenForClassify` holds back a field whose label, description, section's words or options name a sensitive topic (TailorIT's `SENSITIVE_LABEL` terms plus the plan's and the Phase 4 review's shared ones), or whose options are EEO answers ("How do you identify?" with Man, Woman, Non-binary: the Phase 4 review, R4) or hold an EEO decline phrase: it is needs-user (`llm-disabled-for-category`) and never sent. The worker screens the label, hint, section words and options again. `classifyItemOf` sends visible text only (never an aria-label-only label; no upload). `SERVER_CATEGORY_MAP` maps TailorIT's 47 categories, an employment entry's by the section (`sectionKindOf`), the six needs-user ones to needs-user. `judgeClassification` accepts only a category the field's kind holds and whose class lets an LLM classification fill (the profile class), never on a lone checkbox or a field that offers a decline option; the field is then filled from the profile at `CLASSIFY_LLM` (0.70) at most, always flagged (`llm-classified-needs-review`).
    - **The worker** (`src/background/ai-tasks.ts`, `planner.ts`): each run's questions (`RunAi`, in the run's memory only) go out one task at a time, as TailorIT's pick and classify requests, and come back as the core's verdicts. CONTRACT.md "The AI calls as built" has the translation table, the polling rules and the task ids (`RunState.aiTasks` and storage.session's `aitasks`). A task is never cancelled (TailorIT shares one task between callers that ask the same, and prunes it); one found cancelled is asked once more (the Phase 4 review, T-F2). `plan/poll` answers at once: the content script waits between polls. A field asked again after a restart is read by its task id; TailorIT's dedupe key would reattach a running one.
    - **The content script** (`loop.ts`): fields awaiting the LLM are polled (`plan/poll`) between passes, at once when the pass has other work and every `AI_POLL_MS` (1.5 s) when it has none, and their rows and writes arrive for the next pass, which writes them through the same guards as its own. `expired` (or a key the answer leaves out) puts a field back to pending (`requeueAwaiting`), so the next pass sends it in `plan/request` again; any other refusal, and `AI_WAIT_CAP_MS` (125 s) with no answer coming in, leave what still waits blank and flagged `llm-unavailable`. plan/poll never renews the run. A field the pass wrote anyway (a choice's free text typed into it) stops waiting. A dry run waits for its answers too and shows their rows, writing nothing.
    - **Never sent to the LLM:** EEO, legal, salary, consent, country and region values (no pick category; the worker refuses any item outside the list, whose label names a sensitive topic, or whose value or aliases name a sum or, outside a school, a degree and a field of study, a sensitive topic: "Gender Studies" and "United States Military Academy" are names of a field and a school, the Phase 4 review, T-F4), an injected or hard-conflict option, a label hash or a guard flag, hidden text, an aria-label-only label. The e2e marker and privacy tests read every AI request body the mock logged.
    - **The dry-run build** (`TAILORIT_BUILD=worktree`, `npm run build:worktree`): the production build pointed at the worktree's TailorIT on 3300. It was removed when the worktree merged into TailorIT `v1.2`; the dry run now uses the production build (docs/DRY-RUN.md).
  - **As built (Phase 5, the step driver):** `src/content/orchestrator.ts` runs the core's machine (`core/steps/machine.ts`, docs/design/core.md §10.2) and carries out its effects in order; `repeatables.ts` (sections, Add clicks, "I currently work here"), `transition.ts` (the wait for the next step) and the runtime's resume are new. What follows holds over the Phase 2 and 3 notes where they differ.
    - **A run** is `run/begin`, the frame check, then the machine from the run's RunState: `createRunState` and `DETECTED` for a Fill (the launcher or the panel: always a fresh RunState), the paused run's state and `CONTINUE` for Continue after a pause, `RESUMED` by the person after an offer, `RESUMED` by the load after a reload. Every transition that changes the state stores it first (`persist`: `runstate/put` of report.ts `persistedRunState`, never for a dry run), so the step counts and Add counters survive a reload; `report` marks the frame's report due, and it goes out when the run comes to rest (review, the wait for the person's Next, a pause, the policy card, the end), so the side panel sees what a person can act on and `report/put`'s limit is never the run's pace.
    - **scan** is the policy gate (the posting page's and stored copy's states at the run's first scan, this frame's visible text at every one; a hit is `policy-notice` with the card, Fill anyway or Cancel; since v4 no card: the run goes profile-only or AI-anyway), the raw scan and the tripwire, `detectPause` (only once no policy statement waits for the person: `AUTH` for sign-in and account creation, `PAUSE` for an email code, an entry choice or a CAPTCHA; nothing on the step is filled), and `SCANNED` with the step's signature (`core/steps/signature.ts` over the adapter's `stepSignature` parts; one signature for every step without the hook), the policy hits, the tripwire hits new since the last count, and the 32 nearest canaries. A new step's notices start afresh; the run's tripwire, policy and policy-state notices stay. A frame inside another page scans a later step only while it is still seen there.
    - **fill-step** is the repeatable sections, the reactive loop, "I currently work here", the final Guard, then `FILLED` (the keys written, which RunState stores hashed as `storedFieldKey`, and lastFill's counts, a section whose entries are left to the person counted as flagged) or the pause, page-busy or stop the loop ended with. RunState also takes the loop's reveal edges (merged across steps, at most 100) and the tripwire hits a later pass's check found, which the machine has no event for.
      - **Sections:** the adapter's `repeatables` on the step, each kind once. A `plan/request` of no keys names them and brings the worker's entry counts; then, never in a dry run, `addBlocks` clicks each section's Add once at a time (planAdd's counts and the step's stored counters, across reloads; a visible button that `assertNotSubmit` allows, checked before the click is counted; `REPEATABLE`, stored, before each click; a settle after each; a click that added no block stops the adding). A typeless button inside a form is never clicked: the entries it would add are the person's (the `needs-click` notice). An Add button is no field, so it has no row (FillResult.kind has no button kind).
      - **plan/request** over the whole scan lets the worker assign each block its entry afresh (a block our own writes filled then names its entry by fingerprint, so the assignment holds across passes); a typeahead's request over its one field carries the step's latest assignment (`LoopEnv.planSome`).
      - **"I currently work here":** the plan ticks a current job's box (the resolver's check) and never writes its end date; the loop writes a block's box before its end date (`boxBeforeEndDate`); after the loop `planCurrentJob` gives the box and the end date the rows the page left (an end date the page disabled is skipped, `end-date-not-applicable`; one the page still requires and leaves enabled, or still holds, is flagged; a past job's box the page ticked, and its disabled end date, are flagged `unexpected-selection`), never replacing a row of our own write.
      - **Prefill:** a text or date write over what the page put in a field before our first write on the step goes through `reconcilePrefill`: a structured answer is replaced, flagged `prefill-conflict`; prose (a textarea, a free-text question, a job's description) is kept, flagged `prefill-kept`; the same answer written another way (a date by its numbers) is kept, `already-correct`. The planner reconciles choices, boxes, sets and uploads itself.
    - **validate** reads the adapter's visible `errors` (fixable: one on a field no pass of the step planned, or one the page emptied after our write), its `isFinalStep` ('unknown' without the hook), Next's visibility, whether the step settled, and the unconfirmed drafts (`VALIDATED`). A fix pass is the step's fill again, as its next attempt; errors left are surfaced at review with the validation notice, never paused on.
    - **press-next** is the one untrusted `click()` on the adapter's Next, through `clickIfAllowed(…, {allowNext: true})`, only when the reducer asks for it (auto-advance on, the step declared not final and clean, fewer than two attempts); a Next missing, hidden or refused pauses with needs-click (since v4, D7: `NEXT_WITHHELD` `not-pressable`, the run resting for the person's Next). Submit is never clicked: `assertNotSubmit` refuses it with or without `allowNext`.
    - **await-transition** (`transition.ts`) looks again at every page mutation (our own UI aside), every address change (location.ts) and, on the person's turn, their trusted click on Next; each look settles the form root the adapter finds now, then reads the signature: another one is `TRANSITIONED`, the same after the person's Next is `STEP_UNCHANGED`. After our click it is capped (`TRANSITION_CAP_MS`, or the adapter's larger `quirks.transitionCapMs`): `TIMED_OUT` with the signature then (since v4 no pause: the same step validated again, its lastFill cleared, or the new one scanned). With no cap the run rests (`ended` 'waiting'), its report sent and the launcher at review, until the step changes.
    - **Resting:** `FillRun.start()` resolves where the run first rests: 'review', 'waiting', 'paused', 'stopped', 'error' or 'aborted'. At review on an adapter that tells steps apart the run still watches the step, so the person's own Next after fixing what the review showed scans the next step; a Fill replaces a resting run, and Cancel stops one that waits. A pause ends the run: the launcher's or the toolbar's Continue starts the next run from its RunState.
    - **The policy card a later pass's hit showed** (until v4: since D6 there is none) reports `policy-notice` over the machine's own status (the machine has no row for a statement found mid-fill): Cancel ends the run `USER_STOPPED` with `policyDecision` declined; Fill anyway ends it, and a fresh run starts from its RunState (`RESUMED` by the person), whose gate takes the decision (`POLICY_ACCEPTED` without the card). A frame its page hid mid-fill ends `USER_STOPPED` with the form-hidden notice.
    - **After a reload** (`runtime.ts`): with a form root, `runstate/get` for the application, then `resumeMode`. 'auto' (this tab, within `RESUME_AUTO_MS`, not at review): a run starts by itself (trigger `resume`, `RESUMED` by the load) on the step it was on, so its attempts count across reloads and the third visit of a step attempted twice goes to review with `step-retry-limit`, writing nothing. 'continue': a run reports what RunState shows (a pause shows its notice again; review and an older run offer Continue) and rests paused. 'none': nothing. The platform's confirmation page marks the application's RunState done (`SUBMITTED`).
    - **RunState's canaries** are those a plan's answer allowed (`persistableCanaries`, gathered over the run) or a stored RunState held, never one the worker has not checked; a resumed run scans every write for the stored ones too.
    - **The step in the report:** a frame whose adapter tells steps apart reports `step: {index}` (RunState.stepIndex).
    - **After the Phase 5 review (2026-10-31):**
      - **Pauses, everywhere a write or a click happens** (C-S1, X-S1): `detectPause`, over the whole document and its open shadow roots (X-S6), runs at the scan, before every write (`PassEnv.heldBy`, which also refuses a write once another step shows), at validation and right before press-next's click. A pause then gives `AUTH` or `PAUSE`, never a click.
      - **press-next** awaits only the subframe check (a frame inside another page still seen there, else `PAUSE` needs-click with the `next-hidden` notice: X-S8); from there to the click nothing is awaited: the step is the one validated, no pause shows, auto-advance is still on, and no CAPTCHA on the step is unsolved (else `NEXT_WITHHELD`, C-S2, X-S4).
      - **Auto-advance** is the `run/begin` answer's, then each `settings/changed` (a push during the `run/begin` round trip counts too: both must say on).
      - **A step's later attempts in the run** (a fix pass, the step again after Back) start from its earlier attempt's loop books (`LoopEnv.carry`, keyed by the step's signature, with one value hasher for the run), and the person's trusted input is heard for the document's whole life (`touches.ts`): a value they changed since our write is theirs (`changed-by-you`), never written; our verified rows stay ours, and the passes add up (C-S3, C-S5). `fixable` leaves out a field the person emptied themselves. Continue and Fill anyway mid-fill hand the next run this carry (`RunRequest.carry`); a fresh Fill does not.
      - **CAPTCHA at Continue** (X-S4): at a CAPTCHA pause the run notes the widgets the step shows (`captchaElements`); the person's Continue hands them to the next run, which does not pause that step again for them (`PauseOptions.handed`). A solved widget never pauses. Auto-advance still never presses Next on a step whose widget is not known solved (`captchaUnsolved`, `captcha-unsolved`, the `captcha-next` notice): an empty token, or an Arkose widget, which holds none.
      - **The Add click** (X-S3): once counted, the button is checked again at the click itself (still the adapter's Add, connected, shown, enabled, `clickIfAllowed`), with nothing awaited between; a click not made is uncounted after.
      - **A resting run** watches from what the page shows after a step change the machine ignored, and stops watching after `MAX_IDLE_TRANSITIONS` (8) step changes in a row that filled nothing (X-S5).
      - **Cancel at a pause** feeds `USER_STOPPED` (stored and reported stopped) and the runtime drops the run: Continue has nothing left to resume (C-S6). **A run that fails** feeds `ERROR` before it rests (C-S7).
      - **A form drawn late** (v3 Phase 2): an adapter whose page draws its form after the content script starts says so (`quirks.formLateMs`), and the runtime watches the document for its form root until then (`content/late-form.ts`, held at 30 s; a new address starts the watch again); once found, the launcher is mounted, `frame/hello` says the frame has a form, and a stored run is resumed as at start-up. Ashby's does; the generic adapter's and every other one's pages are not watched. Workday's adapter should say so too. While the wait runs only the adapters that say so may claim the page (`lateAdapters`); once it is over, every adapter may. A posting page whose adapter names its posting's text (`postingText`) is watched the same way until that text is drawn, then read for the policy notice. Since jr6 (apply F2 item 5) both watches, this one and a company page's, also hear `class`, `style`, `hidden` and `open` (`SHOWN_ATTRIBUTES`: a form drawn hidden and shown by an attribute alone, as Workable's dialog), counted by `attributesShow`: since the jr6 integration fixes (B3) on a form part itself or a dialog holding one, never on the page's `html` or `body`, and on a wrapper that holds a part only as it turns from drawn hidden to drawn (each watch's own record; once at first sight if drawn), so a scroll class, a carousel or a sticky header holding a search box costs one look, not one per change for the page's life.
- **Adapters:** one interface (`src/adapters/types.ts`).
  - **It covers:** detection and form root, sections, labels and proxies, upload widgets, widgets, busy hints, step signature, Next and Submit (Submit identified, never clicked), final-step recognition, errors, repeatable sections, prefill reading, auth state, and the canonical application URL from which the `applicationKey` is hashed.
  - **The scanner's hooks for what no attribute says** (v3 Phase 2, for Ashby; the generic adapter declares none, so no trap page reads otherwise): `widgetRoots` (more widget candidates, each asked of `widgetFor`), `WidgetDecl.parts` (the native controls inside a declared widget that are its own, never fields: only those, so a control a person can see inside it stays a field), `WidgetDecl.members` (a declared group's buttons, kept to those inside the widget and outside the extension's own UI), `excluded` (parts of the root holding no question, open shadow roots crossed: nothing in them is a field or a flagged upload row; parts and excluded controls are still counted in `hiddenControlsIgnored`), `labelSuffix`, `descriptionFor`, `requiredFor`, `formatFor` (a layout no type or placeholder gives, laid out by the core), `checkedOf` (a radio's or checkbox's chosen state where the page draws it beside the input: the scan's current value, the fillers' already-correct and read-back, `FieldTarget.checkedOf`), `WidgetDecl.optionLabel` (the part of a list's option that is its label), `WidgetDecl.hidesFormWhileOpen`, `WidgetDecl.emptyAfterMs` and `WidgetDecl.highlightIsNoChoice`; `Adapter.uploadState` (a file's upload as the page shows it), `Adapter.postingText` (the posting's own text) and `Adapter.autosaves`; and a fieldset of checkboxes with no legend that the adapter labels (`labelFor`) is one question before any shared name makes one, a group of its boxes, or a lone checkbox when it holds one. `quirks.formLateMs` is the content runtime's (above).
  - **When each one arrives:** Phase 0 ships only the interface. The generic adapter (standard semantics only, final step always "unknown") and the Lever and Greenhouse adapters and URL parsers come in Phase 2, Ashby's in v3 Phase 2 (above, "Ashby"), Workday's later. Each is written from the owner's snapshots and sample URLs, and every DOM hook names the snapshot it came from.
  - **URL parsing:** the extension never calls the contract's `parseAtsUrl`, because TailorIT parses the URL it is sent. A contract-fixture test runs both parsers over one URL table, so platform and job id agree.
    - **As built (the Phase 2 review, W2):** on a host whose platform has a parser (Lever, Greenhouse, Ashby), an address the parser does not read is no application: `applicationOf` answers null (a board, a page after submitting, a `?gh_jid` board, an upper-case `/APPLY`, an Ashby org's board), and the content runtime shows no launcher and runs nothing there. The one exception is the reserved `tailorit-fixture` Lever board (`FIXTURE_BOARD`), which the trap and synthetic fixtures are served on, in every build: it is keyed by origin and path, as is any address on an ATS host with no parser yet (Workday). Ashby's parser reads `/<org>/<posting>(/application)` (the overview is the posting page, `?embed=js` and any query dropped, the org folded), so the reserved `tailorit-fixture` org's synthetic page is an ordinary Ashby posting.
- **UI:**
  - **In-page:** one host element on `document.documentElement` with a closed shadow root, created from the isolated world, so page scripts cannot reach it or patch `attachShadow`.
    - Styles are TS-string constructable stylesheets, falling back to a `<style>` element. There are no CSS files, so no web-accessible stylesheet (since v1.5 the preview page is the one web-accessible resource: "v1.5"), and a strict-CSP fixture checks it.
    - The launcher (32 px, anchored to the form root) handles trusted clicks only, and only while a person can see it (IntersectionObserver v2).
    - Nothing is drawn over the page's fields (the owner's decision of 2026-11-01, docs/SPEC-AMENDMENTS.md §11): the highlights (one outline and chip per status, dashed drafts, the "AI" and "AI guess" outlines, the outlines over Next and Submit) are gone. The review lists every field with its status, its AI mark and its reason; Show on page scrolls to a field and focuses it.
    - The tripwire banner shows the spec's exact sentence (since v4 a branded toast, the one notice D9 keeps). Until v4 the policy card read "This posting says AI-assisted applications are not allowed. Filling is your decision.", with Fill anyway and Cancel (trusted clicks).
    - **As built (Phase 2):** `src/content/ui`, vanilla DOM: `shadow.ts` (the host), `styles.ts`, `launcher.ts`, `notices.ts` (and since the owner's amendments `ai-edits.ts`, `panel.tsx`; since v4 `toasts.ts` in place of `notices.ts`), put together by `page-ui.ts`.
      - **The host:** a zero-size div at the document's origin with inline `!important` styles set through the CSSOM (`all: initial`, `pointer-events: none`, the top `z-index`), registered with `registerOwnUiHost`. Clicks, keys, focus and input events stop propagating at the host, so the page's bubbling listeners never see them. A page that removes the host gets it back three times; after that it stays gone (`content.ui-removed`), and the side panel still has everything.
      - **Styles:** one stylesheet made with the page window's own `CSSStyleSheet` (a sheet may only be adopted in its own document); where that, or `adoptedStyleSheets`, is refused, the same text in a `<style>` element in the shadow root.
      - **The launcher:** 32 px, 8 px inside the form root's top-right corner, measured against the host, so in document coordinates. States and their one action: idle (Autofill), running (none), needs you (Continue after a pause; Open review at the policy card, since the side panel mirrors it), review with the count of rows that need the person (Open review; since v4, D11, its badge shows the percent filled, never a count), error (Fill again; "connect first" when not connected). Open review sends `panel/open` inside the click's own task, with nothing awaited before it. Every handler starts with `if (!event.isTrusted) return;`.
      - **Notices** (until v4; since then the toasts, `toasts.ts`, "v4" above)**:** a stack under the launcher in `COPY.notices` words, each dismissible by a trusted click (the report keeps it); the tripwire notice shows once per run. The policy card holds `COPY.notices.policy`, a line saying when only TailorIT's saved copy said it, and `COPY.policyFillAnyway` and `COPY.policyCancel`.
      - **Seen controls (the Phase 2 review, S1 and S5):** `visible.ts` watches the launcher and the card's two buttons with IntersectionObserver v2 (`trackVisibility`, a 100 ms delay): Chromium's `isVisible`, across frame boundaries, against opacity, filters, transforms other than a 2D move or upscale, and anything painted over the control. Only our own controls are observed, never the page's. The answer is the latest notification's; until the first one after the control was observed or shown, nothing is known, which counts as not visible.
        - A trusted click on the launcher counts only while it is visible: a page layer drawn over it that lets clicks through (a decoy reading "Continue") makes the click do nothing (`content.ui-click-refused`). It must have been seen before the click, because Open review has to reach the worker inside it. A card button just shown is waited for (at most 1.5 s), since its choice needs no gesture; then it counts only if visible and the card is still up.
        - `launcherShown()` is a frame's visibility on its page: a frame inside another page runs only while it is true (the orchestrator's frame check, `content.form-hidden` and the `form-hidden` notice otherwise). A frame with no launcher (the UI failed, or the page removed the host for good) fills nothing inside another page; the top frame fills from the side panel without one.
        - The host carries no transform, opacity or filter of its own (`all: initial`, `opacity: 1`, `transform: none`, `filter: none`), and the anchor's `translateX(-100%)` is a 2D move, so nothing of ours makes `isVisible` false by itself.
        - The costs: a form in a frame scrolled out of view on its page is refused until the person scrolls to it ("Scroll it into view, then choose Autofill again"); a page whose root carries a filter, or that draws something over our launcher at the top `z-index`, gets no launcher clicks; and the answer lags a change by up to the delay, so a decoy put up less than about 100 ms before a click is not seen (a person aiming then was aiming at our launcher).
      - **No highlights** (the owner's decision of 2026-11-01): `highlights.ts` and its stylesheet rules are gone. The Edit with AI buttons are re-measured in one animation frame on any scroll (capture phase), on resize and when the form root resizes.
      - **Words:** every sentence comes from `COPY` (`src/shared/copy.ts`): the launcher's names (`launcherFill`, `launcherRunning`, `launcherNeedsYou`, `launcherPolicy`, `launcherReview`, `launcherError`, `launcherNotConnected`), `dismiss` and `policyFromStoredCopy` (the review's marks `aiChip` and `aiGuessChip`). Its icons, the dismiss button's cross among them, are drawings with no text (Phase F1), so a notice's text is exactly its sentence.
      - **For the e2e suite:** Playwright's selectors cannot reach the closed root. The suite reads it through DevTools (`DOM.getDocument` with pierce) and clicks with the mouse at an element's own box (`e2e/helpers/page-ui.ts`): the launcher is `button.launcher` with its state in `data-state`, a notice `.notice` (since v4 a toast's sentence, `.toast[data-kind]`), the policy card `[role="alertdialog"]` (until v4) with its buttons named by their text. The launcher sits 8 px inside the form root's top-right corner (its centre 24 px in from the right and down from the top).
  - **The report** is one set of presentational React components (`src/ui/report`: props in, callbacks out; React only, no messaging or browser APIs), styled by one TS-string stylesheet.
    - **Where it mounts:** the side panel adopts that stylesheet, and so does the in-page panel (`content/ui/panel.tsx`) inside the closed shadow root, through `adoptedStyleSheets`. The user's setting picks between them; the side panel is the default. (Since v5 Phase 1 the default is the sidebar on the page, the side panel's own views in the top frame's closed root, and the in-page card serves a form inside another page: "v5 Phase 1".)
    - **Rows** show the label, status, provenance, matcher stage, confidence and a reason sentence, grouped by frame and section, with notices on top. Rows carry no value; suggestion labels are fetched only when a row is expanded.
    - **Actions per row:** show on page, confirm draft, save my answer. Saving is offered only after a trusted user edit of a flagged field.
    - **Notices** are mirrored in the side panel, because a page could draw over the in-page panel. In a small embedded iframe the in-page panel collapses to the launcher, which opens the side panel.
    - **Following the tab:** the side panel follows the active tab, and `?tabId=N` pins one. That is also how tests open it as a normal tab.
  - **The options page** (opens in a tab):
    - connection (Connect, pairing code, Disconnect);
    - panel placement;
    - auto-advance, off ("the final Submit is never pressed");
    - pre-approved consent categories, none by default;
    - AI: pick on, classify on, drafts off, with "Your EEO, authorization and sponsorship answers never go to the AI, and questions that look like them are not sent to it.";
    - the two interpretations above, off;
    - anonymous counters, off, listing the five fields;
    - clear saved runs;
    - a first-run disclosure before Connect.
  - **As built (Phase 2):** `src/ui/report` (the report), `src/ui/panel` (the side panel and report page), `src/ui/options`, and `src/ui/page` (what the pages share: checked requests, the connection status, their layout's stylesheet).
    - **Talking to the worker:** the pages take a `PagePlatform`, send page messages through `request()`, which checks every answer against `PAGE_ANSWER_SCHEMAS`, and parse every port message with `PanelPortMessageSchema`. An update that does not parse (a row with any field a report may not hold, a value among them) is dropped whole, and the panel keeps what it showed.
    - **The panel's tab:** without `?tabId=N` it shows its own window's active tab. On each `tabs.onActivated` it reads that tab again rather than taking the activated one, so a tab chosen in another window never takes it over.
    - **The panel's data:** `panel/reports {tabId}` first, then `panel/watch {tabId}` on the `panel` port. A one-shot answer that arrives after a pushed update is dropped as older.
    - **Reconnecting:** when the port drops (a worker restart, or Chrome stopping the idle worker), the panel connects again at once and watches its tab again, which wakes the worker; a frame's report reaches only a connected panel. So an open panel wakes the worker each time Chrome stops it. After more than 5 drops in 10 s it stops and shows a Reconnect button. Timing comes from an injected clock: there is no timer.
    - **The panel's contents:** the connection line (`auth/status`, read again when the tab changes, when the page becomes visible or takes the focus, and after a `not_connected`); Fill, Dry run, Continue and Cancel (since v4 the main button alone: "v4" above); the most urgent run status with its step; what the last button did; then the report. `step.index` counts from 0: index 0 of 6 reads "Step 1 of 6".
    - **The report's layout:** the notices first (since v4 the in-page panel's alone: the side panel passes `notices={false}`, and no policy card is drawn), each code once across frames, in `NOTICE_CODES` order. Each frame whose run waits at `policy-notice` gets a policy card leading them (named by its frame when several wait), with its buttons when the container passes `onPolicyDecision(frameId, decision)`; the side panel sends them as `panel/policyDecision` for that frame. Then each frame by frame id ("Lever form", or "Greenhouse form, embedded in this page"), with its status, step, pause sentence, dry-run note and the hidden fields it left alone, and its rows by section. Opening a row asks for its suggestions' labels, which live only in that open row's state.
    - **Styles:** two TS strings. The report's (`src/ui/report/styles.ts`) is adopted into whatever root the report is mounted in: the document, or a shadow root. The pages' layout (`src/ui/page/styles.ts`) shares its colour tokens and buttons.
    - **The options page:** every change is one `settings/save` of the whole settings. The change shows while it is on its way, with every control held still; then the page shows the settings the answer carries, or the old ones with "Not saved". The pairing countdown repaints on animation frames, which the browser runs only while the page is visible: no timer. The anonymous counters and the offer to save answers arrive in Phase 6; until then each save sends their stored values back unchanged.
    - **Test hooks:**
      - each row: `data-field-key`, `data-status`, and `data-provenance` (`none` when nothing was written);
      - each frame: `data-frame-id`; each notice: `data-notice`; each policy card: also `data-policy-frame`, the frame it decides for; a run status: `data-run-status`;
      - the panel's root: `data-tab-id`; its connection line: `data-connection`;
      - each options input: `data-setting` (`modes.<mode>.<choice>` and `view.<choice>` since v3 Phase 3, before them `autoAdvance`; `panel.side`, `panel.in-page` (`panel.page` since v5 Phase 1), `consent.<category>`, `llm.tiebreak`, `llm.classify`, `llm.drafts`, `interpretations.<name>`);
      - the disclosure: `data-disclosure`; the pairing line: `data-pairing` (`waiting` or `lapsed`).
    - **The capture page** (development builds only) is `src/entrypoints/capture`, with its logic in `src/ui/capture`. [docs/design/runtime.md](design/runtime.md) §11.g describes it as built.
  - **As built (Phase 6, wave 1: the review UI):** what follows holds over the Phase 2 notes where they differ.
    - **The report** (`src/ui/report`):
      - each frame's head shows its run status and step, the step as the adapter's progress line where the report has one (`FrameReport.step.progress`: the adapter's `stepSignature().progress`, visible page text, whitespace collapsed, at most `MAX_STEP_PROGRESS` (120) characters, read at the step's scan), else "Step N"; `RunStatusLine` shows the most urgent run's status and step the same way above the report, in the side panel and the in-page panel;
      - row actions, each drawn only when the container passes its callback: Show on page; the closest options; **Confirm draft** (`onConfirmDraft(frameId, fieldKey)`) on an `llm-draft` row that still needs the person; **Save my answer** (`onSaveAnswer(frameId, fieldKey)`) only on a row the run left `flagged` whose `detail.userEdited` is true (the person changed the field with their own input; the content runtime sets it, wave 2), never on a file, nor on a class whose saved answers are `never` (sensitive, legal, EEO, consent, attention). `actionsFrameId` limits the actions to one frame's rows (the in-page panel's own). `busy` holds them while a request is on its way;
      - an AI draft's row is dashed and marked "AI draft" (`data-draft="true"`); a required field's star is drawn for the eye with "Required" for a screen reader;
      - **the policy notice reads the same in the page and the panel**: when only TailorIT's saved copy of the posting held the statement, the report carries the notice `policy-stored-copy` beside `policy` (the orchestrator adds it at the first scan), and the panel's card, like the page's, says `COPY.policyFromStoredCopy` under its sentence; it is never a notice of its own. The tripwire banner is `COPY.notices.tripwire` in both;
      - the side panel sends Confirm draft and Save my answer as `panel/confirmDraft` and `panel/saveAnswer {tabId, frameId, fieldKey}`, and says what came of each (`COPY.panel.draftConfirmed`, `confirmFailed`, `answerSaved`, `saveFailed`).
    - **The in-page panel** (`src/content/ui/panel.tsx`, the one React file in `content`; `createInPagePanel`; since v5 Phase 1 `panel.ts` keeps its frame and click gate and its views are `card.tsx`, from the sidebar's bundle, so `content` holds no React):
      - the same report components, mounted lazily in the page UI's closed root (React's `createRoot` at the first open, `flushSync` renders, so it shows exactly what it was last given), which adopts `REPORT_CSS` beside the page UI's own sheet; its frame (`.panel`, in `UI_CSS`) uses the report's colour tokens;
      - it sits in the anchor's column under the launcher and the notices (since v4, the toasts move into its column above it), so it never covers the launcher or the policy card (their clicks count only while seen); 380 px wide, at most 70% of the viewport high, scrolling inside;
      - it opens from the launcher's Open review when `settings.panel` is `'in-page'` (since v5 Phase 1 `'page'`, and only in a frame inside another page: the top frame's is the sidebar) (the content runtime reads it from `frame/hello` and each `settings/changed`; choosing the side panel closes it) and the frame has room for it (`panelFits`: the top frame always, an embedded frame from `PANEL_MIN_VIEWPORT`, 420 × 360 CSS px); otherwise Open review opens the side panel as before (`content.panel-collapsed`). Opening sends `panel/shown {open: true}`; its Close button (`aria-label` `COPY.inPage.close`) sends `panel/shown {open: false}`;
      - until the worker's `report/update` arrives it says `COPY.inPage.loading`; a closed panel keeps no report; a `report/update` that does not parse is refused whole (`bad_message`) and the panel keeps what it showed;
      - its row actions are this frame's alone (`actionsFrameId`): Show on page and the closest options run the runtime's own `field/focus` and `field/describe` paths; other frames' rows are shown without actions. It never draws the policy card's buttons: the page's own card has them;
      - **trusted clicks only**: a capture listener on the panel stops every untrusted click before React hears it. Confirm draft and Save my answer (wave 2 passes `confirmDraft` and `saveAnswer`) also need the panel seen at the click (IntersectionObserver v2 on the panel, as the launcher's clicks do), else `content.ui-click-refused`;
      - while it is open the notice stack hides its plain notices (`.notices.with-panel`), which the panel shows; the card stays.
    - **Status colours:** `src/shared/palette.ts`'s (`STATUS_COLOURS`), the report's own; since the owner's decision of 2026-11-01 the review is the one place a status and an AI mark are drawn (no page highlights).
    - **The options page:** "Your fixes", the offer to save answers (`data-setting="offerToSaveAnswers"`, on by default); "Anonymous counters" (below: Telemetry), with its opt-in (`data-setting="telemetry.optIn"`, off) listing the five fields counted (`data-telemetry-fields`), the counts kept in this browser as a table (`data-telemetry="counts"`, or `"empty"`), Export (`a[download]` with a `data:application/json` address built from what the page read: `{fields, counts}`; `data-telemetry-export`) and Clear (`telemetry/clear`). The panel placement's note now says when the side panel opens instead.
    - **Test hooks** added: a row's `data-draft`; a row button's `data-action` (`show-field`, `suggestions`, `confirm-draft`, `save-answer`); the policy notice's `data-notice-source="stored-copy"`; the in-page panel's `data-panel="in-page"`.
    - **The accessibility check:** `e2e/specs/95-accessibility.spec.ts` runs `@axe-core/playwright` over the report page after a trap run and over the options page with counts showing: no serious or critical violation.
  - **As built (Phase 6, wave 2: drafts, saved answers and accepted matches):** contract TailorIT `autofill` `eefe2e4` (`src/contract/tailorit/answers.ts`, the draft route in `ai.ts`). What follows holds over the notes above where they differ.
    - **Saved answers in** (`background/saved-answers.ts`, `profile-cache.ts`): `GET /answers` is read beside `GET /profile`, into memory only, and dropped with the profile; a failed read plans with none. Each is mapped for the core (`optionSetHash ''` is `'none'`, scope `''` global or `<platform>:<board>` a company (`workable:<account>` since API 4), labels never indices, `updatedAt` in ms) and screened again: one whose question names a sensitive topic or reads like an attention check is dropped. Every plan reads them (`PlanInput.savedAnswers`) with the company the frame's address names (`companyOfUrl`: the Lever site or Greenhouse board, lower case; none for an embedded form without its board, Ashby, Workday, Workable (ahead of TailorIT's contract) or the fixture board), so a free-text answer applies only on its own company's forms; the resolver's rules do the rest (the exact key and observed hash, in scope; never EEO, legal, sensitive, consent or attention classes).
    - **Save my answer** (`background/row-actions.ts`): the side panel's `panel/saveAnswer` or the in-page panel's `answer/save`, only for a row the report offers it on and with `settings.offerToSaveAnswers` on. The question is screened first (never read: `sensitive`); a free-text one needs the company its run planned with (`company-unknown`). Then `answer/read` to the frame, and at once `PUT /answers {questionKey: normalizeLabel(label), optionSetHash ('' for none), scope, fieldKind, questionLabel, platform, answer, category}` (`category`: TailorIT's name for the core's, `free_text_question` for free text, else null). Nothing of the answer is kept or logged; the next run reads it back with the profile.
    - **The person's fix** (`content/touches.ts`, `orchestrator.ts`): the touch log records edits (input, paste, drop) apart from key presses and tells a listener of each trusted event. While a run rests, an edit of a field it left flagged sets `detail.userEdited` (the setting on; never a file, nor a value of ours the person changed, which is theirs: `changed-by-you`), and the report goes out again; during a fill, an edit after the fill began and after our last write to the field does the same at the step's end. Turning the setting off takes the marks away.
    - **Drafts** (`core/plan/drafts.ts`, `background/ai-tasks.ts`, `content/orchestrator.ts`): with `settings.llm.drafts` on and never in a dry run, a free-text question the rules recognised, no saved answer fills, with an empty box, a label the LLM may see, no sensitive topic and a `maxLength` of 50 or more (or none) waits for a draft (`ask.drafts`: its key, its visible label, `min(maxLength, 5000)` or null). The worker sends at most three a task to `POST /ai/draft` with the top frame's `postingText` (`''` from a frame that sent none), the lookup's company and job title, the request's canaries and the resume the run attaches; it never sends a question naming a sensitive topic. Back, `drafted` and `trimmed` are a text, and every other reason is why there is none: `draft-empty`, `draft-too-long`, `canary-in-value`, `draft-ai-reference`, `draft-wrapper`, `draft-contact`, `llm-invalid-output`, `llm-disabled-for-category`. planFrame writes a text as an `llm-draft {taskId, confirmedByUser: false}` through `guardWrite` (the canary scan blocks it: `canary-in-value`), at `LLM_DRAFT` (0.70), flagged `llm-draft-needs-confirmation`; a text past the field's cap (its `maxLength`, else 3,000) is `draft-too-long`, and any discard leaves the field blank and flagged with its reason. The frame scans the draft again with every canary it holds before it writes it; the review marks it as the AI's.
    - **Confirming a draft:** `panel/confirmDraft` becomes `draft/confirm` to that frame; the in-page panel confirms in its own frame. The run's waiting draft becomes verified (`ok`, the person's choice), and the report goes out again; the person's own edit of a draft makes the text theirs (`changed-by-you`). Either way the draft is never unconfirmed again in the run: the final Guard reads it as confirmed (`FinalGuardEnv.confirmedDrafts`) and the step's validation counts only drafts that still need the person.
    - **Accepted matches** (`background/match-cache.ts`; core/matcher/cache.ts): `matchcache` in storage.session, loaded into memory once, at most 5,000 least recently used out, passed to every plan as `PlanInput.cache`; a stale entry the cascade found (`PlanOutput.cacheDrops`) is deleted. Each plan names its writes' candidates (`PlanOutput.cacheCandidates`, `cacheEntryFor` with `run-complete`: never an unconfirmed LLM pick, an Other choice, nor an EEO, legal, consent or sensitive field), kept in memory per application (at most 20, 2,000 fields each); a report row that wrote and read back verified keeps a field's candidates standing, any other row (`changed-by-you`, gone, refused, an AI pick or draft) drops them. When the application's RunState reaches done (the platform's confirmation page), the standing candidates are stored. No action lets a person confirm a match, so `acceptedBy: 'user'` is never written.
    - **Counters:** a saved answer's row counts as any written row; a draft that waits for the person is not counted until they confirm it (a success) or edit it (a fail) (`core/telemetry/events.ts` `outcomeKnown`).
    - **Words:** `COPY.reasons` for the draft discards, `COPY.panel.saveOutcomes` for what came of Save my answer, said by the side panel and, under its title, by the in-page panel (`data-panel-feedback`).
    - **Tests:** `src/core/plan/drafts.test.ts` and `accepted.test.ts`; `src/background/phase6-worker.test.ts`, `saved-answers.test.ts`, `match-cache.test.ts`, `ai-drafts.test.ts`; `src/content/phase6.browser.test.ts`; e2e: trap 8 end to end and trap 1's draft clause (`20-traps.spec.ts`), and `57-saved-answers.spec.ts` (a fix saved and reused, a draft confirmed on the report page). The mock answers the new routes (CONTRACT.md "The mock's drafts and saved answers").
    - **Departures:** trap 1's draft clause reads "neither the hidden marker nor the canary" of the request's text: the canary goes in its `canaries` list, which the contract defines for TailorIT to refuse a draft holding one, so the trap invariants set that list aside. Drafts are never asked in a dry run. The extension's own discard checks are the canary scan and the cap, as the brief has it; TailorIT checks AI talk, the wrapper and contact details (its reasons). An ARIA widget's change at rest (a click in its list) fires no trusted input on the field, so it is not seen as the person's; the run reads its values again before the step is left (below, E9).
  - **As built (the Phase 6 review, E1 to E12 and T6-1):** what follows holds over the wave notes above where they differ.
    - **The in-page panel's clicks** (E1, `content/ui/styles.ts`, `panel.tsx`): its rows scroll inside its box (`.panel__root` a flex column, its body `flex: 1; min-height: 0; overflow: auto`, the box clipping what overflows), so nothing of it is drawn outside the box its visibility watch observes. A trusted click counts only when the panel was seen and the click landed on what was seen: its point (a keyboard's click: the control's centre) inside the panel's box, inside the scrolling body for a row's control, and on the control itself.
    - **A closed in-page panel keeps nothing** (E7): closing drops the tab's reports and unmounts its tree; the next opening waits for a fresh `report/update`.
    - **The person's edit** (E3, `content/touches.ts`): a page's own `document.execCommand('insertText')` fires a trusted input event, so an input is the person's edit only when a trusted keydown, pointerdown, paste or drop reached the same element (its target; a label's control for the label) within `INTENT_WINDOW_MS` (1 s) before it; a paste or a drop is an edit itself. Only such an edit marks `userEdited` or makes a value or a draft theirs. A draft whose text still equals the draft as written (its value MAC) stays an unconfirmed draft whatever input reached it. The looser record (any trusted input, keydown, paste or drop) still keeps the loop from writing over a field.
    - **Drafts across runs** (E4, `orchestrator.ts`, `runtime.ts`): the document keeps one value hasher for all its runs and a book of the drafts written in it that still wait for the person (`RunEnv.drafts`: base key and value MAC, memory only). A later run (Fill again, Continue, Fill anyway) rows a field whose value MAC is still its draft's as an unconfirmed `llm-draft` (dashed, Confirm draft, counted by the step's validation). `RunState.drafts` keeps the stored base keys of the drafts still waiting (keys only, at most 100), so a run resumed after a reload rows a field there that holds text it did not write as an unconfirmed draft; a value MAC could not be compared after a reload, since the key never leaves the document's memory. Confirming or editing a draft takes it out of both.
    - **Confirm draft reads the field back** (E6): the field must still hold the draft as written (its value MAC under the document's hasher) and no canary the frame knows; otherwise the row is `changed-by-you` (or blocked `canary-in-value`) and `draft/confirm` answers `expired`.
    - **Save my answer for free text after a worker restart** (E5): the frame's `answer/read` answer names the company its own address names (`adapters/company.ts` `companyOfUrl`), which the worker takes when its memo no longer knows the run's company. Nothing stores the company: storage never holds a page address.
    - **What was written is read again before the step is left** (E9): before our click on Next, and at the person's activation while the run rests (a trusted pointerdown, or Enter or Space), the values the run wrote on the step are hashed again; one whose value MAC is not the one the fill left is rowed `verify-mismatch` and reported, so the accepted-match cache drops its candidates. Drafts are left to the person.
    - **Screened questions** (T6-1): the classify screen (`screenForClassify`: a sensitive topic in the label, hint, section heading or options, an EEO answer or a decline among the options) now also keeps a field from a saved answer (the resolver), from Save my answer (the planner's `savable`, honoured by the frame, and the frame's `answer/read` refusal `sensitive`) and from a draft. The worker screens again at Save my answer: the question's section words, and the answer's own option labels against the EEO answers, the decline phrases and the sensitive terms (`namesUnsavableAnswer`). The sensitive terms gain two sponsorship paraphrases with no topic word ("now or in the future", "support your employment").
    - **Counters** (E8): counted once per run by key, across a step that comes back and a worker restart (`telemetry:counted`).
    - **Posting text** (E11, E12): none from a frame inside another page, and none in a dry run; the lookup's stored posting text never reaches a draft (tests: `content/review-p6.browser.test.ts`, `background/review-p6.test.ts`).
    - **Tests:** `src/core/plan/screened.test.ts`; `src/background/review-p6.test.ts`; `src/content/review-p6.browser.test.ts`; `src/content/ui/panel.browser.test.ts` (E1, E7); the setting-off case of `phase6-worker.test.ts` now asserts on the worker it set (E10).
  - **As built (Phase F1: TailorIT's look):** what follows holds over every note above where they differ. Nothing here adds data: the job card, the page-state strip, Add and Save job, the resume row, the custom question, Edit with AI and the AI-guess marks come later (Phases E and F2), each in a place left for it.
    - **Colours** (`src/shared/palette.ts`, the one source): TailorIT's own, light only. The teal (`BRAND.fill`, #14a1c0) is a background and a graphic (the main button, the launcher's tile, the progress fill); text, icons, links and the focus ring that read as the brand are its ink (#08758c), as TailorIT's own rule says (its teal is 2.97:1 on the canvas). Muted text is #666666 (TailorIT's #7d7d7d is 4.12:1). The statuses take TailorIT's notice tones (success, info, warning, danger, with danger's `line` #f3c2c2), so a status reads the same on the page, in the panels and in the launcher's badge. `palette.test.ts` computes every text pair (4.5:1), the large-text and non-text pairs (3:1), and checks that no stylesheet holds a colour outside the palette and that no `color:` resolves to the teal. Both `prefers-color-scheme: dark` blocks are gone; the pages set `color-scheme: light`. Fonts are the system's (`FONT_STACK`): nothing is fetched. (Since v5 Phase 1 "TailorIT Inter" leads `FONT_STACK`: carried in the extension and added from its bytes to the sidebar's page, the side panel and the options page, so the system font shows elsewhere; still nothing is fetched.)
    - **Icons** (`src/shared/icons.ts`): our own drawings, as data on a 20-unit grid, and TailorIT's mark (`LOGO_MARK`, its `logo-mark.svg`; `LOGO_GLYPH` without the tile). The page's UI draws them with `svgOf` (`content/ui/svg.ts`: `createElementNS` and `setAttribute`, only the shapes and attributes `SVG_TAGS` and `SVG_ATTRIBUTES` allow, so never an `on…`, `href` or `style`); the pages with `<Icon>`, `<LogoMark>`, `<Wordmark>` and `<Brand>` (`src/ui/brand`). Every drawing is `aria-hidden` and not focusable, with no `<title>`: the words beside it say what it means, so a notice's text is still exactly its sentence. No markup builder ships: `scripts/icons.mjs` alone writes the mark as markup, to render it.
    - **The manifest's icons:** `public/icon/16.png`, `32.png`, `48.png` and `128.png`, TailorIT's mark rendered by `npm run icons` (`scripts/icons.mjs`, Playwright's Chromium) and committed; `icons` names the four and `action.default_icon` the 16 and 32 px ones. `check-build` fails a build whose icons are not exactly those four, each a PNG whose header gives its key's size, or whose action names others.
    - **The side panel** (`ui/panel/Panel.tsx`, on TailorIT's canvas #fcfcfc, a column at most 480 px wide), top to bottom: the brand (`h1.tia-brand`: the mark, "TailorIT" with its "IT" in the ink, "Autofill") and a gear to the extension's settings (`a[data-open-settings]`); the connection ("Connected as …" after a green dot; a problem is a strip, `data-tone="problem"`, its link in the strip's own text colour); one strip (`.tia-strip`): no tab (`data-strip="no-tab"`), or the run status line toned by `stripToneOf` (`data-tone`: working pulses, attention, done, neutral); the notices (`ReportNotices`, the policy card first), above the main button; then **Autofill** (`button.tia-cta`, full width, 48 px, white 19px/800 on the teal, 3.05:1 as large text; its name never changes; a spinner while its request is out or a run works, `data-working`), then Dry run, Continue and Cancel as outline buttons (`data-emphasis` on Continue while a run waits for the person, on Cancel while one works; all four disabled only without a tab or while a request is out, as before); what the last button did; **Review** (`section.tia-dashboard`, a white card, its `h2` `COPY.panelTitle`) holding the report without its notices (`Report notices={false}`); and links to the person's TailorIT (`a[data-tailorit-link]`: Settings › Autofill and Jobs). Comments in `Panel.tsx` mark where Phase F2's page strip, job card and custom question go.
    - **The report** (`ui/report`; superseded by v3 Phase 3a's progress and lists, above): after the notices, the completion (`div[data-completion][data-ready][data-needs]`): ready rows of those ready or waiting for the person as counts ("12 of 15 ready", never a percent; "would be filled" in a dry run), a named `progressbar` whose fill's width is set through the CSSOM, how many are left for the person (worded apart from the launcher's count, which adds the Add buttons left), "Required: r of n ready", a count per status (`li[data-tally]`), and "Nothing needs you" only when nothing waits, something is ready, no run works, no notice stands, no frame is at the policy card and it is no dry run. A row that needs the person (`needsPerson`, in `src/shared/report.ts`: it asks for them, or it is flagged or blocked; the launcher's badge counts by the same rule) is an open card (`data-layout="card"`: its icon, label, chip, reason, facts and actions); every other row folds into one line (`data-layout="line"`, `details > summary`: its icon, label, provenance and status in view, the rest a click away; no button in a summary; the chevrons swap by `display`, never a rotation). An AI draft keeps its mark and dashed edge in either layout. A folded row that closes forgets the options it asked for, and a row is keyed by its layout too, so one that turns from a card into a line starts afresh (the Phase F1 review). v2: a row the AI wrote and the run verified (reason `llm-answer-written`, or `llm-draft-written` beside the draft mark) is marked "AI" (`[data-ai-mark="ai"]`), one it guessed (reason `ai-guess`) "AI guess" in amber (`[data-ai-mark="guess"]`), by `aiMarkOf` (the review is the one place it shows); the completion says "N answers written by AI · M are guesses" (`[data-ai-answers]`); a section's heading is its adapter's words, or its slug made readable ("Submit your application"); the launcher's review badge shows "!" rather than a tick while a notice stands. Notices carry `data-tone` (`noticeToneOf`: the tripwire a danger, the frame inside another site's page information, the rest a warning) and an icon.
    - **The page's UI** (`content/ui`): the launcher is TailorIT's tile (32 × 32, radius 9, the glyph on the teal; same anchor, same trusted, seen clicks), its state in a toned badge (`.badge[data-tone]`: "!" for needs you and error, the count for review with "99+" past 99, a tick when nothing needs the person; hidden at rest and while running; since v4, D11, the percent filled, a tick, or "!" for a failure alone) that takes no pointer events, so a click on its overhang is no click on the launcher; while it runs, its glyph hides behind the white spinner. Notices and the policy card (since v4 the toasts, led by TailorIT's mark and name) are white cards with a tone icon; the tripwire's on light red; a notice's dismiss holds a drawn cross. The card is named by its first line, the mark and "TailorIT Autofill" (`aria-labelledby`; a `div`, so its paragraphs are its sentence and the stored-copy line alone), with Cancel (outline) and Fill anyway (the ink). The in-page panel is a white card with a 20 px radius: its head the mark and the name beside a round chevron that still closes and unmounts it (`aria-label` `COPY.inPage.close`), then the status strip and the feedback line, which the report's stylesheet now styles in the shadow root too; its body the report.
